# 🍁 Japan 2027 — Autumn Guide

**Nagoya comparison:** [[Japan 2027 — Nagoya Chapter|Tokugawaen, food, and city photography]]. Autumn color and 2027 event dates need checking; a stay replaces other nights.

**Okinawa comparison:** [[Japan 2027 — Okinawa Chapter|November culture, food, and coastal walks]]. It replaces mainland foliage time; swimming permission and sea conditions remain local dated checks.

**Nara day-trip alternative:** [[Japan 2027 — Nara Chapter|Temples and park/tree photography]]. Use actual foliage, daylight, and closing reports before replacing another Kyoto day.

**Sapporo comparison:** [[Japan 2027 — Sapporo Chapter|Hokkaido city food and seasonal timing]]. Usual city foliage is mid-October to early November; a mid/late-November module trades away Kyoto time without guaranteeing northern colors.

**Fukuoka alternative:** [[Japan 2027 — Fukuoka Chapter|City food, park photography, and hot-pot dinners]]. Compare its route tradeoffs before reducing Kyoto foliage time; future events and colors remain unconfirmed.

**Island alternatives:** [[Japan 2027 — Best Islands]] includes Miyajima and Shodoshima foliage leads and a Naoshima art option; actual color timing and travel-day access need checking.

**Osaka option:** [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Chapter]] adds city shopping/night photography and an optional Minoh foliage walk. Choose a Kyoto day trip or replace two Kyoto nights with Osaka; actual foliage and access need checking. [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Pocket Routes|Open the pocket routes]].

[[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|← Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — November vs May|November versus May]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and reservations]]

**Concept:** November photography, Fuji lakes, low-elevation hikes, matcha/cafés, and your existing shopping interests. **Working length:** 15 days / 14 nights. **Dates/year:** unconfirmed; 2027 is the inherited planning year. No bookings recorded.

## Window to compare

My first window is **mid-to-late November**, positioning Kyoto later in the trip for foliage. JNTO describes November's brisk weather and notes that Kyoto's autumn color season starts around mid-November; actual timing varies by year, site, and elevation. Use annual local foliage reports before assigning temple days. [JNTO November](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/november/).

This guide plans low-elevation walking. Mountain weather/access need separate checks, and November is outside Fuji's normal summit season. Fuji viewpoints remain weather-dependent. There is no guaranteed peak-color or clear-sky date.

## Route

**Tokyo 4 nights → Kawaguchiko 2 → Kyoto 6 → Tokyo 2 = 14 nights.** Same base pattern as scenic summer, making the seasons easier to compare. Choose Kyoto dates around the actual foliage window; compare a KIX departure before committing the final Tokyo return.

Use the summer guide's operator links for Tokyo–Kawaguchiko, the possible Mishima connection to Kyoto, and the Tokyo return. Recheck the November timetable and connection buffer. Kyoto foliage demand can affect hotel availability; compare real quotes rather than assuming this is a cheaper season.

## Fifteen-day autumn outline

These are proposed blocks. Swap foliage days according to local reports and weather; do not stack every temple into one outing. Confirm admission/photography rules separately for each visit.

| Day / night | Main plan | Alternative / evening | Photography focus |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 / Tokyo 1 | Arrive, hotel, dinner, rest | Nearby walk only if energy allows | First autumn city scene |
| 2 / Tokyo 2 | Asakusa and a riverside walk | Café and indoor browsing | Architecture / city layers |
| 3 / Tokyo 3 | Kichijoji park, vintage, coffee, and one record shop | LOFT or gift browsing | Water reflections / leaves if present |
| 4 / Tokyo 4 | Takao Trail 1 if open and conditions suit | Tokyo garden and café alternative | Woodland / temple detail |
| 5 / Kawaguchiko 1 | Transfer; lakeside orientation | Local dinner and early rest | Fuji view if visible |
| 6 / Kawaguchiko 2 | Choose lakeside/Oishi Park plus Chureito OR ropeway | Keep a flexible local weather day | Wide mountain / reflection detail |
| 7 / Kyoto 1 | Transfer on a verified connection; easy evening | Rest | Arrival atmosphere |
| 8 / Kyoto 2 | Nanzen-ji / Eikando area: choose manageable visits | Café; illumination only if announced and booked where needed | Maple/architecture detail |
| 9 / Kyoto 3 | Arashiyama riverside and one chosen temple | Lunch, return, short neighborhood walk | River and hillside color |
| 10 / Kyoto 4 | Tofuku-ji-focused morning; optional Fushimi area later | Reduce walking if queues are long | One autumn composition |
| 11 / Kyoto 5 | Uji tea/matcha outing | Central Kyoto tea/gifts alternative | Tea story / riverside |
| 12 / Kyoto 6 | Daimonji viewpoint OR Kurama–Kibune if access/daylight suit | Flexible foliage catch-up and cafés | Panorama or forest |
| 13 / Tokyo 5 | Return to Tokyo | Easy evening / laundry | Travel transition |
| 14 / Tokyo 6 | Tamiya plus one gift/clothing stop; packing | Donki or earlier shopping lead as time allows | Souvenir story |
| 15 / — | Airport and departure | Protect transfer buffer | Optional final frame |

Kyoto's official [green-maple/foliage spot list](https://global.kyoto.travel/en/faq/detail.php?faq_id=1034) identifies Nanzen-ji, Eikando, Tofuku-ji, and Arashiyama as maple destinations; the route above is my selection from those areas. Specific 2027 illumination periods, night admission, and tickets are unconfirmed. [Arashiyama area guide](https://www.kyoto.travel/en/areas/saga-arashiyama/).

## Walking, clothing, and photo rhythm

[[Japan 2027 — Hiking and Mount Fuji|Existing trail research]] gives Takao, Daimonji, and Kurama–Kibune options. Its spring dates are not autumn reservations. Finish hikes in daylight, check access notices, and adapt to wet/icy ground. Choose a city walk if conditions or your preferred effort make the hike unsuitable.

Pack layers, a warmer outer layer for evenings/lakes, rain protection, and shoes with grip. Plan one main outdoor area and a café break rather than crossing Kyoto repeatedly. My shot sequence: wide scene → leaf/architectural detail → person-scale walking shot with permission → ambient sound. Keep a weather/daylight note in the daily plan once dates are fixed.

## Buying and eating

Autumn is a useful context for browsing your flannel, denim-jacket, and trousers wishlist, but exact stock and size availability still need checking. Use [[Japan 2027 — Clothing to Browse Online]], [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist]], [[Japan 2027 — Kichijoji Shopping and Exploring]], and [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her]] as research references. Old March/April visit dates and October 2026 product stock do not transfer to this itinerary.

Keep matcha/cafés in Uji and central Kyoto, choose one vintage/record outing in Tokyo, and keep Tamiya as the last Tokyo shopping anchor. Her gifts remain capped at US$200 if retained; budget inclusion is handled in [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|the planning note]]. No restaurant or shopping appointment is booked.

# 🏝️ Japan — best islands for your trip

**Klook island tours:** [[Japan 2027 — Klook More Cities and Islands|Island options and the Taketomi/Sado search gaps]]

**Okinawa main-island comparison:** [[Japan 2027 — Okinawa Chapter|Naha, Chatan/Onna, and one Kerama day]]. Compare with Ishigaki/Taketomi or Miyako first; they are separate regional bases.

[[#Souvenirs — small keepsakes and gifts|🎁 Island souvenirs and shopping Maps links]]

[[#Foods to try — island shortlist|🍽️ Island foods and food-stop Maps links]]

**Getting there:** [[Japan 2027 — Island Access from Haneda and Narita|Routes from Haneda and Narita to all nine islands, with rail, flight, and ferry connections]].

[[#Google Maps — sights and arrival points|📍 Google Maps locations]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|← Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Chapter|Osaka chapter]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and reservations]]

**Research checked:** 7 October 2026. **Working year:** 2027, still unconfirmed. This is a selection chapter for your photography/filming, hiking, cafés, food, and gift interests. Recommendations and suggested stay lengths are my judgment; they are not an official island ranking. No island has been added to the dated itinerary or booked.

## My shortlist for you

**Start with Miyajima** for the strongest mix of shrine scenery, a possible hike, food, and a manageable western-Japan detour. **Choose Naoshima instead** if art and architecture are the main reason to visit. **Yakushima** deserves a larger hiking-focused route, and **Ishigaki with Taketomi** works as a dedicated beach-and-village stay. **Enoshima** is the simplest island outing to compare from Tokyo.

Japan's four main islands are major travel regions; this chapter concentrates on smaller island outings rather than treating all of Honshu or Hokkaido as a single stop.

| Island | Best reason to choose it | My suggested time | Fit with your existing route |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Miyajima / Itsukushima** | Shrine waterfront, views, walking, November foliage | One full day from Hiroshima; compare 1–2 nights in the area | Strong first choice for a western-Japan module |
| **Naoshima** | Contemporary art, architecture, coastal compositions | One full island day; preferably a two-night regional block | Good alternative to Miyajima, with timed museum planning |
| **Shodoshima** | Coastal scenery, olive/soy-sauce culture, gorge greenery or foliage | Two island nights | Slower Setouchi alternative; local transport matters |
| **Yakushima** | Mossy forest, cedars, substantial hiking possibilities | 3–4 island nights plus access/buffer planning | A larger route change; prioritize if hiking becomes the trip's focus |
| **Ishigaki** | Ocean views, guided marine activities, stargazing, island base | Four nights as a starting comparison | Domestic-flight module replacing mainland nights |
| **Taketomi** | Red-roof village streets, crafts, relaxed photography | Day trip from Ishigaki, or a deliberately chosen overnight | Pair with Ishigaki; not an independent mainland detour |
| **Miyako Island** | Beaches and ocean scenery across bridge-linked islands | 3–4 nights | Compare against Ishigaki rather than automatically visit both |
| **Enoshima** | Coastal walk, shrine, viewpoint, an easy change of scene | One Tokyo day outing | Least disruption; no extra hotel base needed |
| **Sado** | Historic port streets, rice terraces, coastal scenery, music culture | 2–3 island nights plus access | Better for a northern-Japan route than your current Kansai emphasis |

The suggested nights include room to slow down, not a guarantee against weather disruption. Flight/ferry days consume part of the stay. Actual connections, hours, fares, trail conditions, and 2027 events need checking after dates are chosen.

Confirm the result name and island before navigating, especially **Miyanoura**, which appears on both Naoshima and Yakushima. Island/village overview links orient you to an area rather than an exact entrance.

**Use on the day:** select your actual hotel or starting point in Maps, then compare the proposed directions with the official ferry/bus timetable. A port pin does not establish that the required sailing operates. Confirm your airline's arrival airport on Miyako; the airport link above is one named airport, not an assumption about your flight. For forests and Mount Misen, use official trail maps and access notices alongside the location pin. Check the tide for Angel Road and the Miyajima waterfront; Kabira Bay remains a sightseeing stop with no beach swimming/snorkeling permitted.

## 1. Miyajima — my first choice for this guide

Explore the Itsukushima Shrine waterfront, the town, and a selected temple/park walk. Add **Mount Misen** only if your chosen trail, effort, daylight, and weather suit; a ropeway option still requires checking how much walking remains. In November, **Momijidani Park** is the foliage lead, with actual color timing checked locally. [Miyajima Tourist Association](https://miyajima.or.jp/english/) · [Official Momijidani guide](https://www.miyajima.or.jp/english/spot/spot_momiji.html).

**Photo idea:** compare the waterfront at different tide stages; look up the actual tide times rather than assume one arrival gives both views. Choose shrine/waterfront photography OR a longer Misen outing as the main block. For food, oysters are a regional lead if you enjoy them; decide from the actual menu. [JNTO oyster information](https://www.japan.travel/national-parks/parks/setonaikai/see-and-do/sightseeing-cruise-and-gourmet-oyster-experience/).

**Access:** plan mainland rail to the Hiroshima area, then local travel to Miyajimaguchi and a ferry. A Hiroshima base simplifies luggage and offers a full island day; staying on Miyajima is an alternative if quieter early/late photography is worth the hotel choice. [Official ferry operator](https://miyajima-matsudai.co.jp/en/ticket.html). Exact dated services and any admission/visitor charges remain unquoted.

## 2. Naoshima — art and architecture

Choose a small set of art sites rather than race through every museum: **Chichu Art Museum**, **Benesse House Museum**, and a Honmura/Art House Project block are starting leads. Build the day around ticket availability, then fit coastal walking and lunch around those slots. Chichu and some other facilities currently use dated/timed admission. [Official tickets and facilities](https://benesse-artsite.jp/en/general-information.html).

**Access:** compare **Uno Port**, reached via the Okayama area, with **Takamatsu Port**. Decide your accommodation base before matching ferry arrivals to museum tickets. The official access page contains mixed-year notices, so check the linked operator's actual travel-date timetable. [Benesse access](https://www.benesse-artsite.jp/en/access/index.html).

**Photo idea:** coastline, architectural lines, and permitted outdoor art. Check each facility's photography/filming rules; do not build your film around unrestricted museum interiors. **Teshima** is a possible second island day only if you replace other activities and verify its museum slot and ferries. No art festival has been assumed for 2027.

## 3. Shodoshima — slower scenery and food culture

My two-day split is **Olive Park / food-culture browsing** on one day and **Kankakei Gorge** on another. Add **Angel Road** only when the tide and local access information support the visit; it is a tidal sandbar, not a guaranteed all-day walkway. The island is known for olives, soy sauce, and somen, with gorge greenery and autumn foliage offering different photo subjects. [JNTO Shodoshima guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/sg/jbyj-blog/shodoshima/).

**Gift idea:** a small olive-based product or packaged local food, chosen within the existing shopping cap and checked for your luggage/import needs. **Access planning:** identify the exact ferry port, accommodation, and onward local bus/taxi/rental plan before booking. Widely separated stops should not be treated as one compact walking route. [Local tourism association](https://shodoshima.or.jp/).

**Photo idea:** sea and grove textures in May; gorge color in November when reports support it. Prefer this over Naoshima when scenery and food culture matter more than museums.

## 4. Yakushima — the hiking-focused choice

Start by comparing a **Shiratani Unsuikyo** forest route with the shorter walking options at **Yakusugi Land**. Select an actual mapped course by effort; these names cover different lengths and terrain. A Jomon Sugi outing is a separate strenuous planning decision, not a casual extra after a forest stroll. [Town trail access information](https://www.town.yakushima.kagoshima.jp/soshiki/kanko_machizukuri/kankosushin/kankosite_English/2/756.html) · [Official hiking guidance](https://www.yakushima.or.jp/assets/file/guidance/tozan_guide_en.pdf).

**Photo idea:** moss detail, cedar scale, flowing water, and forest sound. Rain protection for you and the camera matters; check the actual trail/weather notices, particularly for higher routes. A wet island forecast is not a reason to assume every forest trail remains suitable.

**Access:** investigate air connections and sea access via Kagoshima for the chosen dates; match the actual arrival point to your accommodation and trail transport. [JNTO island-access overview](https://faq.japan-travel.jnto.go.jp/en/guide/island-hopping/). I would reserve 3–4 island nights plus mainland/connection buffer as a starting comparison. Rebuild the 14-night route around it instead of stacking it onto Fuji, Kyoto, Osaka, and another island module.

## 5–6. Ishigaki and Taketomi — beach base plus village day

**Ishigaki:** compare staying near the southern town/port for meals and ferry access with a quieter coastal base that needs a more deliberate transport plan. Starting leads are **Kabira Bay scenery**, one coastal viewpoint, and a guided marine outing suited to your experience. **Swimming/snorkeling from Kabira Bay's beach is not permitted**; sightseeing there and an operator-approved marine trip elsewhere are different activities. [Official Ishigaki guide](https://visitokinawajapan.com/destinations/yaeyama-islands/ishigaki-island/).

**Taketomi:** use a separate ferry day for the red-tiled village, streets, crafts, and shore views. The island's minsah weaving is a gift-browsing lead; inspect the actual product and price. Respect residential areas when filming. [Official Taketomi guide](https://visitokinawajapan.com/destinations/yaeyama-islands/taketomi-island/).

**My four-night starting outline:** arrival/orientation → Ishigaki coastal day → Taketomi village day → guided marine outing OR weather-flexible land day → departure. Swap the middle days when operators and conditions justify it. This includes only one neighboring island, not a promise that every ferry excursion runs.

**Access and season:** compare actual flights to Ishigaki, local airport/port travel, and dated ferry services. Warm southern-island scenery does not guarantee beach conditions in May or November. Check wind, waves, and operator advice before choosing water activities. Weather can interrupt flight/ferry plans; leave a mainland buffer before an international departure. [Official Okinawa sea guidance](https://visitokinawajapan.com/wp-content/themes/visit-okinawa_multi-language/lang/en/assets/document/plan-your-trip/useful-information/303/All%20About%20the%20Sea%20in%20Okinawa.pdf).

## 7. Miyako Island — compare if beaches are the priority

Miyako is my alternative to Ishigaki when the trip's purpose is beach scenery and a relaxed coastal stay. Nearby islands connected by bridges add different viewpoints without making each outing a ferry day. Ishigaki is my first comparison if the Taketomi village visit and ferry-based exploration matter more. [Official Miyako Islands guide](https://visitokinawajapan.com/destinations/miyako-islands/) · [JNTO Miyako overview](https://www.japan.travel/en/destinations/okinawa/Okinawa/miyako-island/).

**Plan:** choose one coastal area per day and compare rental car, taxi, bus, or a tour against your actual accommodation location. No car or driving eligibility has been assumed. Follow current beach notices and operator advice for swimming. **Photo idea:** a wide water-color scene, coastal detail, and a permitted viewpoint; do not stop on a bridge roadway for a shot. Give this 3–4 nights before deciding whether the travel cost is worthwhile.

## 8. Enoshima — simplest from Tokyo

Use a Tokyo outing for the shrine area, coast, and **Sea Candle** viewpoint if operating. Choose a manageable island walk with breaks; stairs and an optional cave visit can change the effort. A full Kamakura sightseeing circuit plus every Enoshima stop would make a different, busier day. [Official Fujisawa access and attractions](https://discover-fujisawa.jp/en/access/).

**Photo idea:** coastal layers and later light when weather and your return plan suit. A Fuji view is a bonus, not a guarantee or a replacement for your planned Fuji-lakes visit. Check [official night-event notices](https://discover-fujisawa.jp/en/special/night-event/) before relying on an illumination; current events are not confirmation for 2027.

**Route fit:** replace one Tokyo outdoor block, such as the optional Takao day, and return to the same hotel. This is the easiest first island choice when you want to preserve all 14 nights and the Kansai plan.

## 9. Sado — save for a northern route

Explore **Shukunegi's historic port streets**, coastal scenery, and selected rice-terrace or cultural stops. Taiko culture is another reason to investigate it; a future festival date or performance requires its own confirmation. [JNTO Sado guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/destinations/hokuriku-shinetsu/niigata/sado-island/).

**Access:** compare travel to Niigata and the actual ferry route, then establish local transport. A car can help with dispersed sights, but has not been assumed; check a workable bus/taxi/tour alternative if you are not driving. Suggested time is 2–3 island nights plus connections. For your present Tokyo–Fuji–Kansai focus I would put Miyajima or Naoshima ahead of Sado. [Sado tourism](https://www.visitsado.com/).

## Foods to try — island shortlist

**Added / checked:** 7 October 2026. These are local dish ideas, not confirmed travel-date menus or restaurant bookings. My first three for you are **anago-meshi on Miyajima**, **Shodoshima somen**, and **Yaeyama soba on Ishigaki/Taketomi**. Choose one main meal and one small sweet per island day; an Ishigaki beef meal can be a deliberate splurge after checking its price.

| Island | What to try | What it is / how I would order | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Miyajima** | **Anago-meshi**, oysters, **momiji manju** | Grilled conger eel over rice as your main meal; cooked oysters as an alternative or small plate. Finish with a maple-leaf-shaped cake; fillings can include red bean, custard, chocolate, or matcha. | [Local savory dishes](https://www.miyajima.or.jp/english/gourmet/gourmet.html) · [Sweets and fillings](https://visit-miyajima-japan.com/en/culture-and-heritage/culinary-heritage.html) |
| **Naoshima** | Udon lunch | A practical Kagawa noodle lunch between art visits; Kinosaki Udon is a named lead. This is a regional food choice, not a claim that udon is unique to Naoshima. | [Official tourism restaurant listing](https://naoshima.net/ja/foodsen/kinosaki-udon/) |
| **Shodoshima** | **Hand-stretched somen**, local soy sauce / olive products | Thin wheat noodles; try fresh/unprocessed somen if on the menu. Browse a small soy-sauce or olive product as a gift rather than buy a heavy basket. | [Official somen guide](https://www.my-kagawa.jp/en/see-and-do/10126) · [Local products](https://www.kagawa-culture-compass.net/en/spot/052.html) |
| **Yakushima** | **Tobiuo — flying fish** | Ask for the restaurant's cooked flying-fish dish or set meal; preparation and availability depend on the menu. A prearranged bento is practical for a chosen hike. | [JNTO Yakushima food guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/ma_229/) |
| **Ishigaki** | **Yaeyama soba**, **Ishigaki beef** | Regional noodles in broth for one lunch; compare a clearly priced beef portion or set meal for your special dinner. Check the actual toppings and broth if you avoid an ingredient. | [Regional soba explanation — MAFF](https://www.maff.go.jp/e/policies/market/k_ryouri/search_menu/5566/index.html) · [Okinawa beef brands](https://visitokinawajapan.com/discover/food-and-longevity/taste-okinawa/tender-tasty-premium-meats/) |
| **Taketomi** | Yaeyama soba | The same regional noodle family makes a sensible village-day lunch. Choose an open café/restaurant and leave enough time for the ferry; no named venue or stock is confirmed here. | [Official regional cuisine guide](https://www.maff.go.jp/e/policies/market/k_ryouri/search_menu/5566/index.html) |
| **Miyako** | **Miyako soba**, mango dessert, optional Miyako beef | Noodle soup first; mango shaved ice or juice if available. The official guide lists fresh local mango season as June–August, so May/November availability should not be assumed. | [Official Miyako food guide](https://visitokinawajapan.com/destinations/miyako-islands/miyako-island/) |
| **Enoshima** | **Shirasu-don**, shirasu croquette or crackers | Rice topped with tiny whitebait fish; ask for the cooked version if that suits you. Raw whitebait depends on the day's catch. A croquette is an alternative snack. | [Official Fujisawa local cuisine](https://discover-fujisawa.jp/en/sightseeing/cuisine/) |
| **Sado** | **Buri katsudon**, local rice and seafood | Deep-fried amberjack over rice; choose a local rice/seafood meal if that dish is unavailable. | [JNTO Sado food information](https://www.japan.travel/en/destinations/hokuriku-shinetsu/niigata/sado-island/) |

### Food stops with Google Maps links

These are Google Maps searches for verified browsing leads or named food areas; check the displayed island/branch and current menu before visiting. No travel-day hours, prices, reservations, or opening status have been confirmed.

- **Miyajima — Momiji-do Head Shop:** an age-momiji (deep-fried momiji manju) snack lead. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Momijido+Head+Shop+Miyajima+Hiroshima) · [Tourism association's food/shop map](https://www.miyajima.or.jp/english/map/img/map_omotesando.pdf).

[Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Nakabuan+Shodoshima+Kagawa) · [Official somen-experience information](https://www.my-kagawa.jp/en/see-and-do/10126).

### Sweets and gifts for her

**My first pick is a small momiji-manju box** if you visit Miyajima. Taste one before deciding on the filling, then check the package's best-before date and storage instructions against your return date. Keep fresh desserts as something to enjoy on the island; a packaged gift needs its own shelf-life check. Shodoshima's small olive-food products or dried somen are alternatives for someone who enjoys cooking.

Take-home gifts count under shopping and her existing US$200 cap; meals/snacks eaten during the trip count under meals. They share your existing allowances rather than creating another food-gift budget. Link back to [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her|her gift shortlist]] when recording a purchase.

**Photography idea:** a wide meal scene → one food texture → preparation only with staff permission. Check your meal options before the island return connection; don't assume a late ferry arrival leaves dinner venues open. Food preferences and allergies have not been supplied, so the shortlist is flexible.

### Your spending and packing plan

My suggested **island souvenir target is up to US$30–50**, carved out of the existing shopping allowance. This is a planning limit, not a claim that any specific craft costs that amount. Choose one keeper and one small gift; compare actual prices before deciding.

Her souvenir, beauty product, or sweets share the existing **US$200 gift cap**. If that cap stays inside your US$300 shopping allowance, only US$100 remains for all your other purchases, including Tamiya and clothing. An island accessory replaces another planned fashion gift; olive cream replaces another beauty gift. The earlier decision about whether her gift cap is inside the main allowance remains open.

For food, check the label, storage needs, expiry against your return date, and applicable import requirements. For ceramics, ask for protective packaging. Liquid beauty products and bottles need packing according to the actual airline/airport rules; no baggage or customs entitlement is assumed here. Keep receipts and record the purchase once in [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist]] or [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her]], as appropriate.

## Summer versus November versus May

These are my selection priorities, not weather forecasts or guaranteed foliage/swimming windows.

| Season | First comparisons | What changes the decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Summer | Ishigaki/Taketomi or Miyako for a dedicated coastal stay; Yakushima if forest hiking dominates | Heat, rain, sea conditions, transport disruption, and actual activity availability; marine wildlife sightings are never assured |
| November | Miyajima; Shodoshima if gorge scenery matters; Naoshima for art | Current foliage reports, daylight, museum opening calendars, and sea-access conditions |
| Second half of May | Miyajima, Naoshima, or Shodoshima for scenery; Yakushima for mapped forest routes | Actual weather, holiday demand, trail access, and the balance of green landscapes versus city time |
| Any of your three concepts | Enoshima as one Tokyo outing | Travel-day weather, attraction access, and a verified return connection |

## Fit one island module into 15 days / 14 nights

The existing summer/autumn daily tables remain the base concept. Choose one module before creating a revised calendar. The following night counts are deliberate alternatives, not additions to a booked trip.

| Alternative | Nights | Concrete tradeoff |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enoshima outing | Existing Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 6 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Replace one Tokyo outing; no new hotel base |
| Miyajima module, Tokyo return | Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 4 → Hiroshima area 2 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Two Kyoto nights replaced; use a full Hiroshima-based day for Miyajima. Osaka can remain an optional Kyoto day trip, replacing another outing. |
| Art-island module, Tokyo return | Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 4 → Uno/Naoshima area 2 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Two Kyoto nights replaced; one full Naoshima day. Choose the actual lodging base and ferry before tickets. |
| Miyajima plus Osaka, KIX departure | Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 3 → Hiroshima area 2 → Osaka 3 = **14** | Reduces Kyoto to three nights and removes the Tokyo return; move Tamiya into the first Tokyo block. Requires comparing actual international flights. |

**Two-night Miyajima module in the Tokyo-return variant:** Days 1–6 Tokyo/Fuji as before; Day 7 Kyoto arrival; Days 8–10 your three selected Kyoto outings; Day 11 travel to Hiroshima-area accommodation; Day 12 Miyajima; Day 13 Tokyo transfer; Day 14 final shopping/packing; Day 15 departure. Hiroshima city sightseeing needs its own time allocation rather than being implied by the accommodation base. Rail legs and luggage arrangements need quotes.

Yakushima, Sado, and the Okinawa islands deserve a fresh route comparison once selected. The Fuji summit itinerary also needs a separate rebuild: preserve its hut, descent, and recovery blocks instead of borrowing nights invisibly.

## Budget, filming, and final checks

The current ceiling remains **US$5,000 in Japan, excluding international flights**. Island accommodation replaces accommodation elsewhere. Record domestic flights/ferries, local transfers, rental/tour costs, baggage, museums, and chosen activities; no actual island quote proves that a module fits the existing category allocations. Souvenirs share your shopping allowance, and her gifts still share the US$200 gift cap. Track everything in [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings]].

My film outline is **arrival crossing → wide island scene → one cultural/nature detail → meal/craft with permission → closing coast/forest scene**. Use [[Japan 2027 — Photography and Filming Wishlist]] and respect museum, shrine, shop, and residential restrictions.

- [ ] Select one island module and the main purpose: scenery, art, hiking, or beaches.
- [ ] Confirm year/dates, departure airport, and which mainland nights/outings it replaces.
- [ ] Match timed admissions to the exact dated ferry/local-transfer plan.
- [ ] Check tides for tidal viewpoints, and current trail or sea conditions for the activity chosen.
- [ ] Compare full costs, cancellation terms, luggage arrangements, and a weather fallback.
- [ ] Save your return connection and avoid relying on a same-day remote-island transfer before the international flight.

**My next comparison for you:** Miyajima versus Naoshima. Choose Miyajima for shrine/coastal/nature photographs; choose Naoshima for art and architecture. Enoshima can remain a separate Tokyo outing if another city activity is replaced.

## Secondhand alternatives to revisit

Used garments may have shrunk; their flat measurements matter more than the letter label. Use the existing [SAFARI official directory](https://e-safari.co.jp/) for your optional Koenji vintage outing, choosing an exact listing and branch before calling anything available in your size.

## How to check a candidate yourself

1. Open the Japan product link; use browser translation if helpful.
2. Select the **color and size** and inspect the current stock message. サイズ means size; カラー means color.
3. Open the size chart, サイズ表. Compare body and finished-garment measurements separately: 身幅 is flat garment width; 肩幅 is shoulder width; ウエスト is waist; 股下 is inseam.
4. For a branch visit, use **店舗在庫状況 / 店舗の在庫を確認する** (store inventory), choose the exact store, and record the result and date. A generic nearby-store message without a selected branch is inconclusive.
5. Recheck close to your visit; try on L/XL tops and the candidate trouser sizes where fitting is available. Check the applicable return terms before paying.

## Route and budget

For new basics, use one UNIQLO stop on an existing Ginza-area outing if convenient. On **7 April**, Tamiya remains the suggested Shinbashi anchor; clothing shopping is an optional extension after checking the hotel route and time to pack. Vintage shopping on **30 March** replaces the blossom buffer and remains optional.

Your existing **US$150 clothing cap** is unchanged. The list offers alternatives across all requested categories; buying every row is not assumed to fit the cap. Convert actual yen totals with your rate/fees, then choose which pieces matter most before using the remaining allowance. Tamiya purchases come from the separate suggested souvenir split in [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist]].

## Spending targets

The **US$40 daily food/drinks target × 15 dates = US$600** already in the guide. It is a personal allocation, not a menu quote or total daily trip cost. Record actual purchases in yen and convert using your actual rate/fees.

Transport, admissions, and shopping vary by day: use the existing trip pools of **US$200 local/airport transport**, **US$250 intercity rail**, **US$250 attractions/experiences**, and **US$300 shopping/miscellaneous**. Hotel, connectivity/insurance, and reserve stay in the full budget. These cards add no new budget allowance. Replace estimates with actual quotes in [[Japan 2027 — Bookings]].

# 🍜 Japan 2027 — Fukuoka chapter

**Klook tours to compare:** [[Japan 2027 — Klook More Cities and Islands#Fukuoka|Fukuoka tour and experience options]]

**Next regional comparison:** [[Japan 2027 — Sapporo Chapter|Sapporo / Hokkaido]] — summer parks, Otaru, and optional hot springs. Choose regional nights deliberately before combining Kyushu and Hokkaido.

[[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|← Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — Fukuoka Pocket Routes|Three pocket routes]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and bookings]]

[[#Google Maps — sights shopping and transport|📍 Google Maps]] · [[#Foods to try and a yatai evening|🍜 Food]] · [[#Getting here — Haneda Narita and western Japan|🛬 Getting here]]

**Research checked:** 7 October 2026. **Working year:** 2027, unconfirmed. This is an optional city chapter, with no dates, hotels, tickets, or restaurants booked. Routes and stay lengths are my recommendations for your photography, food, tea/cafés, vintage clothes, hobby browsing, and gift interests. Current sources do not confirm 2027 stock, prices, event dates, or travel-day opening times.

## My starting recommendation

Compare **three Fukuoka nights**: arrival and a relaxed Hakata evening → Ohori Park, tea, and Tenjin/Daimyo browsing → **Dazaifu OR Itoshima** → departure. Four nights make room for both excursions or a slower city day. With two nights, prioritize the city and one yatai evening rather than assume two full excursion days fit.

Fukuoka sits on **Kyushu**, west of your Osaka chapter. It earns its place when you want another food-focused city or a gateway to a future Kyushu trip; adding it to 15 days means reducing other bases. It is not scheduled as a casual Kyoto day trip.

## Explore by neighborhood

| Area | Your main reason to visit | How I would use it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Hakata Station / Old Town** | Arrival base, food and souvenirs; Kushida Shrine and Tochoji | A manageable history walk on a city day; shorten on arrival |
| **Nakasu / Kawabata** | River views, night photography, yatai | One evening with a selected food stop and river walk |
| **Tenjin / Daimyo** | Vintage clothes, independent shops, collectibles | Your main shopping block, with café breaks |
| **Ohori / Maizuru** | Pond reflections, park walking, art and tea | Main morning outing plus seated tea; one indoor museum option |
| **Dazaifu** | Shrine setting, approach shops, a traditional sweet, museum option | Separate outing; do not combine with the Itoshima coast |
| **Itoshima / Futamigaura** | Coastal scenery and sea-facing torii / rocks | Separate weather-dependent excursion with planned transport |

The city tourism authority identifies Daimyo as a vintage/independent-shopping area and Hakata Station's complex as a food/shopping hub. [Official shopping guide](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/articles/detail/408a917e-7d53-48b8-99ee-e3127e3fdb7b). Use [Hakata's official historic walking course](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/en/route/detail/37ca2359-b761-4c73-8063-f1232dc84ef7) to choose a few stops, rather than treat every temple as compulsory.

## Foods to try and a yatai evening

My first meal is **Hakata tonkotsu ramen**, then one yatai evening and a **mizutaki OR motsunabe** dinner on another night. Taste one sweet during the Dazaifu outing if chosen. Prices, portions, restaurant hours, and dietary preferences are still open.

| Dish | What to expect | My suggested role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Hakata tonkotsu ramen** | Pork-bone broth ramen | Arrival/city lunch; choose a menu and queue that suit the day |
| **Mentaiko** | Seasoned pollock roe | Try a small portion with rice or another clearly described dish |
| **Motsunabe** | Offal hot pot with vegetables | One dinner if you enjoy offal; check minimum portions for a solo diner |
| **Mizutaki** | Chicken-and-vegetable hot pot in chicken broth | Dinner alternative if offal is not appealing |
| **Yakitori / skewers** | Grilled skewers; Fukuoka offerings can include pork and vegetables as well as chicken | Choose by ingredients rather than assume every skewer is chicken |
| **Umegaemochi**, Dazaifu | Grilled mochi with sweet red-bean filling | One freshly made sweet on the shrine approach |
| **Yame tea** | Regional tea; compare the actual serving menu | Seated Ohori break, paired with a small meal or dessert if available |

[Official Fukuoka food overview](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/f-food.html) · [Hot-pot explanation](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/en/articles/detail/f8bce646-da5f-4661-8339-8c6764e76a2e) · [Skewer customs](https://gofukuoka.jp/mustdo/) · [Dazaifu sweet explanation](https://www.kyuhaku.jp/en/visit/visit_pguide.html).

### Choose a yatai area

**Yatai are compact food stalls**, a different format from a full indoor izakaya. For you, I would compare **Tenjin** after shopping and **Nakasu** for river/night photos; **Nagahama** is a third option if its location and menus suit your day. Choose one area for dinner rather than schedule a three-district crawl. [Official yatai area comparison](https://gofukuoka.jp/en/articles/detail/ff5d9c6d-860e-4ec5-952f-366c1b2b60fe).

Use the [city's yatai guide](https://gofukuoka.jp/YATAI) and [English stall map](https://gofukuoka.jp/yatai/img/yataimap_eng.pdf) to pick a stall closer to the travel date. The map is an orientation source, not confirmation that every stall will operate that evening. Weather and individual schedules can change the plan.

**My evening plan:** look at the displayed menu/prices → choose one stall with a suitable seat → order a small meal/drink → ask before filming → make room when finished → take your river photos afterward. Keep an indoor restaurant alternative if stalls are closed or queues are unsuitable. [Official yatai dining context and limited-seat etiquette](https://gofukuoka.jp/en/articles/detail/d773d813-d160-4b3f-8092-8f7c2fe5d17d).

Your Osaka izakaya plan remains a separate experience. Fukuoka's first food priority in this chapter is a yatai evening; it does not require buying a guided food tour.

## Tea, shopping, and hobby browsing

| Lead | What I would browse / try | Verified source and location |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Ohori Terrace / & LOCALS** | Yame tea, a seated café meal, packaged tea/food gift browsing | **1-9 Ohori Koen**, near the Japanese garden. [Current city guide](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/en/articles/detail/f590b215-77b4-4324-8fc8-eb189717744d) |
| **Daimyo** | For you: tee, denim jacket, flannel, or trousers, chosen by condition and fit | Area-level lead; no particular vintage shop inventory confirmed. [Shopping overview](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/articles/detail/408a917e-7d53-48b8-99ee-e3127e3fdb7b) |
| **Tenjin malls / independent shops** | For her: a small accessory or a garment after size confirmation; compare Y2K/Korean-inspired styles if present | Fashion browse, not a verified current product list or 2027 trend forecast. Use the current tenant directory on arrival. |
| **Mandarake Fukuoka** | Used toys/collectibles, manga, CDs/DVDs; choose one item by interest and condition | **Grand Building, 2-9-5 Daimyo, Chuo-ku**; current official hours 12:00–20:00. [Official branch](https://www.mandarake.co.jp/dir/fko/index-en.html) |
| **THE GUNDAM BASE FUKUOKA, Canal City** | Optional Gunpla browse if Gundam models appeal to you | [Current mall branch listing](https://canalcity.co.jp/shop/detail/10210401?tenant_id=10210401). Check access notices and actual item stock. |
| **JR Hakata City / station shopping** | Final packaged sweets and compact gifts | Convenient station-area shopping; check the exact counter and hours for the travel day. [City shopping guide](https://www.gofukuoka.jp/articles/detail/408a917e-7d53-48b8-99ee-e3127e3fdb7b) |

The Canal City Gundam shop and Mandarake are different stops; neither is confirmation of a Tamiya item or a substitute for your Tokyo Tamiya Shinbashi anchor. No Fukuoka record-specific store or perfume counter has been verified in this chapter. For clothing, your L tops / EU 44–46 trousers are size references, not guaranteed Japanese fits. Her size is still needed before choosing fitted garments.

### Souvenirs for you and her

- **Hakata Torimon:** white-bean-filled sweets with a Western-style butter/cream influence; a small box is my first sweet-gift comparison for her. [Manufacturer product description](https://www.meigetsudo.co.jp/info/wp-content/themes/meigetsudo/pdf/202408.pdf). This brochure is dated 2024; use it for the product description, not current prices, pack sizes, or stock.
- **Menbei:** crunchy mentaiko/seafood-flavored crackers; a savory gift alternative. [Manufacturer English product guide](https://www.fukutaro.co.jp/wordpress/wp-content/themes/fukutaro/English.pdf). Older price/tax details in the brochure are not a current purchase quote.
- **Packaged Yame tea:** browse Ohori Terrace's retail selection; confirm tea type, pack size, and price. A good personal/café-memory souvenir without another clothing-size decision.
- **One vintage piece or compact collectible:** replaces another personal shopping choice. Do not add a new city allowance simply because another store is available.

Check food ingredients, storage instructions, and best-before date against your return date. Her purchases share her existing US$200 cap and the unresolved relationship to the main US$300 shopping allowance. [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her|Gift wishlist]] · [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist|Personal shopping]].

## Dazaifu or Itoshima — choose the third-day outing

**Dazaifu:** Nishitetsu Fukuoka (Tenjin) → **Nishitetsu Futsukaichi** → Dazaifu Line → **Dazaifu Station** → shrine approach on foot. Subway Tenjin and Nishitetsu Fukuoka (Tenjin) are distinct stations requiring a walk. [Official heritage access guide](https://www.dazaifu-japan-heritage.jp/en/access/). Plan shrine/approach, lunch, and umegaemochi; add the **Kyushu National Museum** only if its opening calendar and your pace suit. [Museum visitor guide](https://www.kyuhaku.jp/en/visit/visit_pguide.html).

Check [Dazaifu Tenmangu's current official notices](https://www.dazaifutenmangu.or.jp/) before visiting. Shrine restoration/access arrangements have changed; old images of a temporary sanctuary do not establish what will be open in 2027. No specific sanctuary layout, bridge access, or museum exhibition is promised here.

**Itoshima / Sakurai Futamigaura:** choose one coastal area, a meal/café, and your photo walk. [Official prefectural Futamigaura guide](https://www.crossroadfukuoka.jp/fr/spot/12456). Compare **Showa Bus West Coast Liner** from the Hakata/Tenjin area with other dated local options; check the exact boarding point and final return. The operator has published changes affecting the **Futamigaura (Meotoiwa-mae)** stop; the current 2026 timetable uses nearby **PALMBEACH** and **Tsuki-to-Taiyo Itoshima-Sabo** stops. [2026 operator timetable](https://www.showa-bus.jp/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/0_04_westcoastliner_20260401.pdf) · [Operator notices](https://www.showa-bus.jp/2025/08/) · [Operator homepage for updates](https://www.showa-bus.jp/).

Treat those stops as research leads, not 2027 confirmation. Coastal spots are dispersed; a train ride to the Itoshima area does not by itself complete the journey to Futamigaura. A rental car/taxi/tour is an alternative only after a quote and a deliberate choice; driving has not been assumed. A sunset shoot depends on the actual last return transport, so choose an earlier photo session if necessary.

For nature without an excursion, use **Ohori Park and an optional Maizuru walk**. This chapter proposes park/coastal walking, not an unverified mountain-hiking route. Your existing [[Japan 2027 — Hiking and Mount Fuji|hiking chapter]] remains the place for the researched trail options.

## Summer, November, and May

| Season | How I would adapt Fukuoka |
| --- | --- |
| **Summer** | Short park/old-town walks when comfortable, indoor shopping and seated breaks midday, then yatai if operating. Keep the coast weather-dependent; no fireworks or festival has been assigned. |
| **November** | Park/temple photography, a warmer layer for evening stalls, and a hot-pot dinner. Foliage and night events need the actual year's reports; this is not a replacement for Kyoto's foliage plan. |
| **Second half of May** | Park greenery and a coastal outing when conditions suit. Recheck the actual calendar, lodging prices, and excursion transport before choosing dates. |

An example of date-sensitive research is the **2026 Hakata Old Town Lightup Walk**, announced for October 31–November 3. It is **not a 2027 booking opportunity or evidence that mid/late-November dates include the event**. [Current official announcement](https://gofukuoka.jp/events/detail/187d3a5a-4626-457d-9076-3a655f7dc8b8). Keep future events optional until announced.

## Photography and filming

My Fukuoka sequence: **station arrival → old-town architectural detail → tea beside the pond → a clothing/collectible detail with permission → yatai preparation with permission → Nakasu river reflections**. The coast alternative uses a wide sea/torii composition, rock/water detail, and a walking transition. The Dazaifu alternative uses the approach, shrine detail where permitted, and a freshly made sweet.

Use [[Japan 2027 — Photography and Filming Wishlist|your existing 30–90 second outline]]. Ask before recording staff, diners, shop interiors, or food preparation. Follow shrine/museum restrictions and keep walkways and stall seating usable. No drone, tripod placement, or commercial filming permission has been arranged.

## Getting here — Haneda Narita and western Japan

| Starting point | Route to compare | Key checks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Haneda (HND)** | Domestic flight HND → Fukuoka Airport **FUK** → Airport Line subway from the domestic terminal to Hakata/Tenjin → hotel | Actual dated flight, baggage, terminal, check-in, and hotel route. If connecting after international arrival, follow the airport/airline arrival-transfer procedures. |
| **Narita (NRT)** | Domestic flight NRT → FUK → subway from the domestic terminal → hotel | Compare the dated same-airport flight and full baggage cost. Do not silently switch the departure to Haneda; changing airports requires its own ground transfer. |
| **Kyoto / Osaka** | Kyoto or Shin-Osaka → Tokaido/Sanyo Shinkansen to **Hakata**, direct or with a change as required → hotel | Hakata is the Fukuoka city Shinkansen anchor; check the actual service, luggage arrangements, and total fare. |
| **Hiroshima / Miyajima module** | Return to Hiroshima Station → Sanyo Shinkansen to Hakata → hotel | This is a western continuation after the island module; preserve its full island day and recount hotel nights. |
| **Tokyo city, rail alternative** | Tokyo/Shinagawa → Tokaido/Sanyo Shinkansen to Hakata, changing if required | Compare total travel time and price against flight plus airport access; it is a substantial transfer day. |

[JAL domestic search](https://www.jal.co.jp/jp/en/dom/reservation/) · [Peach route/schedule search](https://www.flypeach.com/en/lm/st/routemap) · [JR Central](https://global.jr-central.co.jp/en/) · [JR West](https://www.westjr.co.jp/global/en/). These are routes to compare, not verified date-specific flight or train availability.

**At Fukuoka Airport:** the **domestic terminal connects to the subway**. If arriving at the **international terminal**, compare its ground transport or the airport's shuttle to the domestic terminal before taking the subway. [Official airport access](https://www.fukuoka-airport.jp/en/access/). Choose the terminal shown on your itinerary; a pin at the airport is not the whole hotel transfer.

**Where to stay:** compare **Hakata** for rail arrival/departure and station food, or **Tenjin** for Daimyo shopping and the Nishitetsu Dazaifu start. No hotel quote has been obtained. Select the property by actual station exit, room/air-conditioning needs, total price, and cancellation terms. For a late yatai evening, check the last subway or budget a local taxi explicitly.

## Fit Fukuoka into 15 days / 14 nights

These alternatives leave the existing seasonal daily tables unchanged until you choose one. Counts refer to actual accommodation nights.

| Concept | Night count | What you give up / need to compare |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Tokyo return, Fukuoka module** | Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 3 → Fukuoka 3 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Replaces three Kyoto nights. No Osaka overnight block; an Osaka day trip would replace one of the reduced Kyoto outings. FUK → Tokyo flight/transfer needs pricing. |
| **Osaka plus Fukuoka, departure via FUK** | Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Kyoto 3 → Osaka 2 → Fukuoka 3 = **14** | Reduces Kyoto and removes the final Tokyo return. Move Tamiya into the first Tokyo block. Compare the actual international itinerary from FUK, including connections; no direct long-haul departure is assumed. |

**Tokyo-return outline:** Days 1–6 unchanged Tokyo/Fuji → Day 7 Kyoto arrival → Days 8–9 choose two Kyoto outing blocks → Day 10 travel to Fukuoka / easy Hakata evening → Day 11 Ohori, tea, Tenjin/Daimyo, one yatai area → Day 12 Dazaifu OR Itoshima → Day 13 Tokyo return → Day 14 final shopping/packing → Day 15 international departure. Three Kyoto nights cannot preserve the six-night Kyoto sightseeing table.

If you want **Miyajima, Osaka, Fukuoka, Fuji, and a full Kyoto program**, compare more nights or explicitly remove other blocks; do not stack every chapter's alternative. The separate Fuji summit branch also needs a fresh count that retains its hut and recovery days. [[Japan 2027 — Best Islands|Island options]] · [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Chapter|Osaka alternatives]].

# 🍜 Fukuoka — pocket routes

[[Japan 2027 — Fukuoka Chapter|← Full chapter, official sources, and Google Maps]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget / reservations]]

**Undated cards:** proposed outing blocks, not calendar bookings or opening times. A three-night stay has an arrival day, two main outing days, and departure. Use Cards 1–3 in that order if arrival allows, reducing Card 1 when needed. Four nights can accommodate both Card 3 alternatives; with two nights, reduce the program.

## Card 1 — arrival, Hakata, and a relaxed evening

**Anchor:** hotel near Hakata or your selected city base.

| Block | Plan |
| --- | --- |
| Arrival | Airport → correct terminal transport/subway → hotel; or Hakata rail arrival → hotel. Confirm luggage arrangements. |
| If daylight and energy allow | Choose Kushida Shrine OR a short old-town walk; leave closed interiors for another day. |
| Optional browse | Canal City / Gundam Base only if enough time and current access suit; otherwise omit. |
| Dinner | Hakata ramen or another nearby meal. Nakasu photos are optional if you have energy and a return plan. |

**Food goal:** one ramen meal. **Shot:** station transition → architecture detail → first food scene. **Buy idea:** none required on arrival. **Fallback:** food, rest, and a short hotel-area walk. Do not force a late-arrival temple/shopping/yatai circuit.

- [ ] Arrival terminal/station, hotel route, and late check-in confirmed.
- [ ] No extra luggage-carrying sightseeing assumed before hotel arrangements.
- [ ] Tomorrow's main block and weather checked.

## Card 2 — Ohori tea, Daimyo shopping, and yatai

**Anchor:** Ohori Park → Tenjin/Daimyo → one dinner area.

| Block | Plan |
| --- | --- |
| Morning | Manageable Ohori pond walk; optional Maizuru extension if energy/weather suit. |
| Break | Ohori Terrace / & LOCALS tea or meal if open; use an alternative café when needed. |
| Afternoon | Daimyo clothes browsing plus Mandarake if collectibles matter; choose two browsing stops rather than shop everything. |
| Evening | Tenjin yatai for convenience OR Nakasu for river atmosphere. Confirm stall operations and check the menu/prices. |

**Food goal:** Yame tea and one yatai meal. **Buy idea:** one vintage garment OR collectible; packaged tea is an alternative gift. **Shot:** pond reflection → tea detail → clothing texture with permission → night street/river. **Rain alternative:** Fukuoka Art Museum if open, selected shopping, and an indoor dinner. **Summer adjustment:** shorten park walking and use seated/indoor breaks.

- [ ] Shop hours, condition, actual size, and total cost checked.
- [ ] Ask before filming inside the café/shop/stall.
- [ ] Last subway or paid taxi alternative settled before dinner.

## Card 3 — choose Dazaifu OR Itoshima

### A. Dazaifu — culture and a sweet

**Route:** Nishitetsu Fukuoka (Tenjin) → Nishitetsu Futsukaichi → Dazaifu Station → walk the approach. Allow for the walk between subway Tenjin and the Nishitetsu station if arriving by subway.

**Main block:** shrine approach and Dazaifu Tenmangu → lunch → freshly made umegaemochi. **Optional:** Kyushu National Museum if open and enough time remains. Check the shrine's current access notices; do not rely on old temporary-sanctuary photos. **Return:** verified Nishitetsu service, then hotel; hot-pot dinner optional after checking portion requirements and price.

**Shot:** approach wide view → permitted shrine detail → sweet close-up. **Buy idea:** one small packaged gift only after checking expiry/storage. **Rain adjustment:** museum plus a shorter shrine/approach visit when conditions suit, or replace with an indoor city day.

### B. Itoshima — coast and photography

**Route:** verify a Showa Bus West Coast Liner or another workable dated route to the Futamigaura area and back. Confirm your actual boarding/alighting stop; older Meotoiwa-mae listings may not match current service. Maps pins alone do not establish a bus journey.

**Main block:** one Futamigaura coastal area → meal/café → a short photo walk → return to the city. **Shot:** sea/torii wide scene → rock/water detail → walking transition. **Buy idea:** none required. **Sunset:** optional only when an actual return service or separately arranged ride supports it. **Bad-weather alternative:** city cafés/art/shopping or Dazaifu when appropriate.

- [ ] Pick A or B; do not combine them in one day.
- [ ] Opening/access notices and actual outward/return connections checked.
- [ ] Daylight/weather and meal options fit the chosen transport.
- [ ] Any taxi, tour, car, museum, or shopping cost fits the shared budget.

**Departure day:** hotel → Hakata or the correct Fukuoka Airport terminal with a reliable buffer. No last-minute excursion is scheduled before the onward train/flight. For Tokyo-return routes, the separate Tokyo hotel nights remain part of the 14-night count.

# 🩺 Japan 2027 — Health preparation

**Maps:** [[Japan 2027 — Maps|📍 All destination, shop, café, and transport searches]]

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|← Dashboard]] · [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials|Travel essentials]]

**Official guidance checked: 6 October 2026.** General preparation for the proposed two-week Tokyo–Kyoto trip; personalize it with a clinician and recheck guidance in 2027. These steps reduce risk but cannot guarantee you will avoid illness.

## 📅 Book a pre-travel appointment

- [ ] Arrange a pre-travel consultation **at least a month before actual departure**, earlier if follow-up is needed. Season/month are undecided as clarified 8 October 2026; the earlier February example does not set the appointment date.
- [ ] Bring vaccine records, medication names/doses, allergies, and the itinerary, including transit countries and any rural outings.
- [ ] Discuss ongoing conditions, recent symptoms, walking demands, and long-flight suitability.
- [ ] Ask which examination or tests are appropriate for your history; do not assume a broad blood-test package is necessary.
- [ ] Plan any overdue dental care early enough to recover before travel.

CDC recommends a consultation at least a month before visiting Japan. Earlier review helps if vaccine series or an existing condition need attention. [CDC Japan guidance](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/destinations/traveler/none/japan) · [Pre-travel consultation framework](https://www.cdc.gov/yellow-book/hcp/preparing-international-travelers/the-pre-travel-consultation.html)

## 💉 Review vaccines with your clinician

- [ ] Check routine protection, especially **measles/MMR**, tetanus-containing vaccines, and seasonal influenza; review current COVID-19 recommendations for your age and health.
- [ ] Discuss **hepatitis A and B** based on prior vaccination, health, age, and activities.
- [ ] Reassess if the itinerary changes to longer rural stays, camping, or higher-risk activities.

CDC does not recommend Japanese encephalitis vaccination for short trips limited to urban areas. Rural exposure can change that assessment. Recommendations are individualized and differ from border-entry requirements. [Current CDC Japan vaccine recommendations](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/destinations/traveler/none/japan)

## 😷 Reduce respiratory infections

Keep vaccinations current, wash hands regularly, favor well-ventilated spaces, and consider a well-fitting high-filtration mask in crowded indoor places, airports, and public transport. If sick, reduce contact with others and seek advice promptly if you are at higher risk of severe illness. [CDC respiratory-illness prevention](https://www.cdc.gov/respiratory-viruses/prevention/index.html)

## ✈️ Prepare for the long flights

Move your legs and do calf exercises while seated; walk periodically when safe. If you have a previous clot, recent surgery, pregnancy, or another clot-risk factor, ask your clinician about tailored prevention and whether compression stockings are appropriate. Do not start aspirin or blood thinners for a flight on your own. [CDC long-distance travel and blood clots](https://www.cdc.gov/yellow-book/hcp/travel-air-sea/deep-vein-thrombosis-and-pulmonary-embolism.html)

Keep arrival day light, allow sleep and regular meals, and build walking tolerance before the trip. Comfortable, already-tested shoes and breaks fit the guide’s photography days better than an ambitious first-day schedule.

## 🍱 Food, outdoors, and allergies

Wash hands before eating; choose food prepared and stored safely. If pregnant, immunocompromised, or otherwise vulnerable to foodborne illness, discuss avoiding raw or undercooked foods with your clinician. Use sun protection and prevent insect bites during outdoor outings. [CDC Japan health precautions](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/travel/destinations/traveler/none/japan)

If you have seasonal allergies or asthma, agree on a symptom plan before departure. Record food allergies in Japanese and ask restaurants about ingredients and cross-contact rather than assuming a dish is suitable.

## 💊 Medicines and a small travel kit

- [ ] Check **each active ingredient**, quantity, and any required permission against Japan’s medicine-import rules before packing, including over-the-counter products.
- [ ] Keep regular medicines in labeled original packaging in hand luggage, with a prescription/clinician letter and a supply covering the full journey and foreseeable delays, within permitted limits.
- [ ] Ask a pharmacist about suitable pain/fever relief, any allergy treatment, and oral rehydration sachets; pack plasters/blister dressings, a thermometer, masks, and hand sanitizer.
- [ ] Confirm insurance medical coverage, exclusions for existing conditions, and the assistance number.

Japan allows up to a month’s supply of many ordinary prescription medicines without Import Confirmation, but controlled or prohibited ingredients have separate rules. A foreign prescription alone does not establish permission. [Official Japanese medicine-import guidance](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/kenkou_iryou/iyakuhin/kojinyunyu/topics/tp010401-1_00001.html)

## 📞 If you become unwell

Use [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials#📞 Contacts to save offline|the contacts card]] and your insurer to find suitable care. Severe breathing difficulty, chest pain, fainting, or a severe allergic reaction needs urgent assessment; in Japan call **119** for an ambulance. Tell clinicians about recent international travel. [JNTO emergency contacts](https://www.japan.travel/en/plan/hotline/)

## 📝 Personal checklist — complete privately

- **Appointment date / clinic:** TBD.
- **Vaccine review and follow-up:** TBD.
- **Medication import check completed:** TBD.
- **Allergy / ongoing-condition plan:** TBD.
- **Insurance assistance / coverage checked:** TBD.

Keep detailed diagnoses, prescriptions, and personal medical records in a private note if sharing the travel guide.

# 🌿 Japan 2027 — Hidden gems and slower discoveries

**Maps:** [[Japan 2027 — Maps|📍 All destination, shop, café, and transport searches]]

[[Japan Travel Guide 2027|← Full travel guide]] · [[Japan 2027 — Today|📱 Today]] · [[Japan 2027 — My Five Things|💛 My priorities]]

**Sources checked: 6 October 2026.** These are lesser-headline alternatives, not secret or guaranteed crowd-free places. Choose one or two across the trip. Suggested durations exclude travel and are planning allowances. Current operating rules below need rechecking for March–April 2027.

## 🧭 Where they fit

| Place | Choose it for | Suggested itinerary slot | Time to allow on site |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Kiyosumi Gardens, Tokyo | Pond reflections, stones, and a slower photo walk | 30 March or 7 April · replace an optional outing | 60–90 minutes |
| Fukagawa Edo Museum, Tokyo | Recreated historic streets indoors | A rainy Tokyo half-day; pair with Kiyosumi only if weather allows | 60–90 minutes |
| Kagurazaka backstreets, Tokyo | Street details, cafés, and a relaxed meal | 27 March · optional afternoon after Chidorigafuchi, if energy allows | 90 minutes–2 hours |
