# 📷 Japan 2027 — Photography and filming wishlist

**Hiking and Fuji:** [[Japan 2027 — Hiking and Mount Fuji|Fuji viewpoints, Mount Takao, and Kyoto hiking options]]

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|← Dashboard]] · [[Japan 2027 — Daily Pocket Cards|Daily cards]] · [[Japan 2027 — Today|Today]] · [[Japan 2027 — Memories|Keep your favorites]]

**Creative plan · added 7 October 2026.** Based on the proposed 25 March–8 April itinerary. Light suggestions are creative preferences, not promised conditions or entry times. Check access, weather, flowering, and local signs; choose one photo and one short sequence each day rather than collecting everything.

## My three priorities

- [ ] **A blossom photo I love:** one strong wide scene, reflection, or close detail.
- [ ] **A matcha memory:** tea color, a cup in my hands, and a sentence about the taste.
- [ ] **One 30–90 second travel film:** blossoms, city/temple details, tea or food, and a personal moment.

These support [[Japan 2027 — My Five Things]]; a finished personal memory counts even if nothing is posted.

## Chidorigafuchi

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-03-27|27 March]]; a Tokyo buffer can replace it.
- [ ] Photo: blossom branches framing the moat; try one reflection and one close detail.
- [ ] Film: a steady wide view, water movement, then a blossom close-up; 5–10 seconds per clip.
**Light:** soft morning light or overcast for details; reflections depend on wind. Keep evening footage optional until illumination is confirmed.

## Shinjuku Gyoen

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-03-28|28 March]].
- [ ] Photo: garden-wide context, then a branch against a clean background.
- [ ] Film: a stationary garden view and blossom movement from a permitted spot.
**Light:** morning within verified access hours; gentle backlight if available. A handheld session keeps the kit simple; see the venue rules below.

## Nakameguro

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-03-29|29 March]].
- [ ] Photo: river perspective, reflections, and neighborhood textures.
- [ ] Film: one river-wide clip and a nearby detail; record only from a place where stopping is allowed.
**Light:** late afternoon; optional blue-hour/night footage only if access and seasonal lighting are confirmed. Move off busy bridges before composing.

## Philosopher’s Path

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-01|1 April]] or a swapped Kyoto day.
- [ ] Photo: canal leading lines; add petals on water only if conditions provide them.
- [ ] Film: steady water, a tree detail, and a short personal reaction from a clear standing spot.
**Light:** soft morning light; overcast can suit blossom detail. Protect the relaxed 2–3 hour photo block.

## Historic Kyoto

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-02|2 April]].
- [ ] Photo: an allowed Kiyomizu-dera architectural composition, then one historic-street detail.
- [ ] Film: a wide view and a texture/detail without blocking steps or foot traffic.
**Light:** morning within visiting hours; choose architecture and textures if blossoms disappoint. Gion footage depends on local permissions and signs.

## Fushimi Inari

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-03|3 April]].
- [ ] Photo: torii repetition or a close architectural detail from an allowed public position.
- [ ] Film: one stationary corridor view and one detail; skip a shot if it would interrupt worship or obstruct movement.
**Light:** try gentler morning light; the full mountain route is optional. No empty-corridor promise.

## Asakusa

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-03-26|26 March]].
- [ ] Photo: temple/streetscape context and one small detail; Sumida Park is an optional existing extension.
- [ ] Film: a street-wide clip and a purchased snack close-up where permitted.
**Light:** easy morning session; shorten it for jet lag. Do not add a separate outing just for footage.

## Matcha and shops

**Slots:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-04|optional Uji on 4 April]], existing café stops, or [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-07|7 April shopping]].
- [ ] Photo: matcha color beside natural window light; souvenir and hands; one personally meaningful record or vintage find.
- [ ] Film: tea preparation only if staff agree, cup-to-table detail, and a tasting reaction. For shops, choose an approved detail or record the purchase back at the hotel.
**Light:** natural window light when available; protect the experience and meal time. Uji remains one excursion choice, replacing Nara/Osaka.

## Tokyo buffer

**Slots:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-03-30|30 March]] and [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-07|7 April]].
- [ ] Revisit one missed blossom shot or make a small neighborhood story in the single area chosen that day.
**Light:** choose from current weather and bloom reports. A skyline shot stays optional; visibility and access decide.

## Kyoto buffer

**Slot:** [[Japan 2027 — Pocket 2027-04-05|5 April]].
- [ ] Revisit the strongest blossom scene; if choosing Arashiyama instead, frame bamboo from the permitted path and capture one steady clip.
**Light:** soft light when available; return with time to pack. The alternative replaces the blossom outing.

## Travel moments

**Slots:** arrival, the two intercity transfers, and departure.
- [ ] Packing detail, a window landscape if practical, and a short arrival/return voice note.
Avoid showing passport, boarding-pass codes, reservation numbers, or private screens. Filming is optional; transfers and recovery take priority.

## A simple film sequence

1. **Opening:** a blossom scene or first-impression voice note.
2. **Tokyo:** one wide street/river view and one detail.
3. **Journey:** a brief window view or packing transition.
4. **Kyoto:** canal or temple context, then a detail.
5. **Tea and a personal moment:** matcha and one sentence about the experience.
6. **Ending:** favorite scene and a closing thought.

For each chosen scene, aim for a **wide view → medium view → detail**, holding clips about 5–10 seconds with a little stillness before/after the action. Capture a short clean ambient sound where recording is welcome. Use vertical framing for your primary social clips; take a separate horizontal view only if you want a landscape version. Keep recording choices consistent with a kit tested before departure.

Livestreaming is a separate optional activity: choose a permitted location, test the connection/audio, and set a short time limit. Recording footage does not establish permission to broadcast it.

## Permissions and venue checks

**Shinjuku Gyoen:** the official page distinguishes personal non-commercial filming from commercial work, restricts obstructive professional equipment for specified photo activities during busy seasons, and says monetized livestreaming is not permitted. Check the exact activity and equipment rules before visiting. Its current 2027 restriction listing pairs dates with inconsistent weekdays, so reconfirm the dated notice rather than treating it as a final calendar. [Official photography/filming rules](https://policies.env.go.jp/national-garden/shinjukugyoen/english/photography/image_capture/)

**Kyoto/Gion:** respect designated photography areas, private property, and street access. Do not follow or photograph geiko/maiko without permission or block streets. [Kyoto City visitor guidance](https://kyoto.travel/en/news/sightseeing-guidelines/)

For each café, shop, temple, or other venue, check its own rules; asking about photos does not establish permission for video, livestreaming, commercial work, or a tripod. Until confirmed, mark permission as **not checked**. Sources checked 7 October 2026; recheck before travel.

### Permission record — copy per venue

- **Place / date:** …
- **Intended use:** personal photo / recorded video / public post / monetized content / livestream …
- **Equipment:** handheld / grip / tripod / microphone …
- **Status:** not checked / allowed with conditions / declined …
- **Rule link or staff response, checked on:** …
- **Allowed area, time, and conditions:** …
- **Alternative if declined:** another permitted shot or a voice note later …

## Choose today’s shots

Copy your choices into [[Japan 2027 — Today]]:
- **Photo I most want:** …
- **Short sequence:** wide … → medium … → detail …
- **Light / access / permission checked:** …
- **Favorite file and story:** …

Each evening, keep one favorite photo and one useful clip in [[Japan 2027 — Memories]]. Back up originals separately from the camera card/device and verify the copies before deleting anything. Editing and posting can wait until home.

# Japan — preparation before choosing dates

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|Dashboard]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials|Detailed essentials]]

**Research checked: 8 October 2026.** You clarified today that the travel season and month are undecided and the flight searches were tests. The March–April dates are a sample blossom itinerary. The note names retain 2027 for continuity; the actual year, length, travelers and rooms remain to be confirmed.

## Choose the experience before the calendar

For your photography, matcha, cafés, vintage clothing, records and gift interests, the main seasonal decision is the scenery and walking conditions. The recommendations below are my judgments. Climate patterns do not establish weather, blossom peaks or leaf color on a particular future day.

| Window to investigate | What it offers for your interests | What preparation changes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Late March–early April | Tokyo/Kyoto blossom photography; the existing dated guide is an example | Layers, movable photo sessions, crowd-aware pacing and dated garden/event checks. [JNTO March](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/march/) |
| Second half of May | Green gardens, city walking, cafés and shopping; a useful balanced alternative | Check local rain patterns, especially if adding Okinawa. Tokyo/Kyoto sakura generally has finished; Golden Week is a separate busy period. [JNTO May](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/may/) |
| June | Hydrangeas and indoor culture; Hokkaido offers a regional alternative with comparatively less rain | Rain protection for you and camera gear, indoor fallback blocks and flexible outdoor outings. Less rain is not a guarantee of dry weather. [JNTO June](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/june/) |
| July–August | Festival and summer outings; investigate this branch if a Fuji summit matters | Heat/humidity pacing, lighter clothing, rests and route-specific mountain preparation. Annual festival schedules and Fuji access need separate verification. [JNTO July](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/july/) · [Official Fuji season](https://www.fujisan-climb.jp/en/season/) |
| September | A later-summer alternative with room for coastal or regional travel | Keep buffers around flights/ferries and review disruption cover: JNTO identifies late August/September as a frequent typhoon period. [JNTO September](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/september/) |
| Mid/late November | Autumn temple photography and cooler walks; a strong alternative to blossoms | Warm layers, earlier outdoor finishes and fresh leaf-color reports. Kyoto's season generally starts in mid-November; that is not an exact-year peak forecast. [JNTO November](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/november/) |

JMA independently describes large regional climate differences, early-summer Baiu rain and Okinawa's rainy-season onset in early May. Its overview supplies climate context, not a forecast for your trip. [Japan Meteorological Agency climate overview](https://www.data.jma.go.jp/cpd/longfcst/en/tourist_japan.html)

**My starting comparison:** second-half May versus mid/late November for relaxed city exploration; retain March–April if sakura is the main goal, and investigate summer if the Fuji summit is important. No season is established as cheaper without comparable dated flight and hotel totals.

**2027 holiday dates are already published:** April 29 and May 3–5 fall in the Golden Week period; November 3 and 23 are holidays too. Holidays are established calendar facts, while the effect on prices and crowds needs dated checks. [Cabinet Office official 2026/2027 holiday list](https://www8.cao.go.jp/chosei/shukujitsu/gaiyou.html)

## Prepare now, whatever month you choose

1. **Documents:** check passport nationality, type and expiry for each traveler. MOFA currently lists Chile for eligible short stays, normally 90 days, but leaving from Chile does not establish your nationality. Transit eligibility follows the actual connection route. [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials#🛂 Visa check — passport confirmation pending|Existing checklist]] · [MOFA visa exemptions](https://www.mofa.go.jp/j_info/visit/visa/short/novisa.html).
2. **Medicines:** make a private list of active ingredients and quantities. MHLW permits up to one month's supply of many ordinary prescription medicines without Import Confirmation; controlled ingredients have separate rules. Some medicines are prohibited despite a foreign prescription. Where controlled-drug permission applies, NCD says to apply at least 14 days before travel; start earlier to resolve questions. [MHLW ordinary medicines](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/kenkou_iryou/iyakuhin/kojinyunyu/topics/tp010401-1_00001.html) · [NCD controlled medicines](https://www.ncd.mhlw.go.jp/en/application2.html).
3. **Phone and payments:** check eSIM compatibility, bank verification abroad, backup card/cash and offline access to the vault. Practice downloading maps and translation languages now; test the actual functions offline. Use [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials#📱 Phone and payment backups|the existing device checklist]]. These are preparation tasks, not claims that your setup has been tested.
4. **Walking and camera routine:** test shoes, comfortable carrying weight and a rain cover; establish a simple daily photo-backup routine. Keep planned outings modest enough for breaks. Your existing [[Japan 2027 — Photography and Filming Wishlist|photo/film wishlist]] can be reused in any season, with local access and light checked later.
5. **Insurance:** compare medical assistance, existing-condition exclusions, disruption terms and cover for the activities you actually choose, especially hiking or marine outings. JNTO recommends adequate travel insurance and provides visitor emergency guidance. [JNTO safety guidance](https://faq.japan-travel.jnto.go.jp/en/plan/emergencies/) · [[Japan 2027 — Health]].
6. **Luggage:** measure your bag and decide whether you prefer carrying or forwarding between bases. Forwarding is an available option, but hotel acceptance, delivery timing and price need confirmation for your route. Keep essentials with you while a bag is in transit. [JNTO luggage and transport guidance](https://www.japan.travel/en/responsible-travel-guide/features/traveling-comfortably/) · [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials#🧳 Tokyo–Kyoto luggage decision|Existing rail baggage checklist]].

## Costs and procedures worth knowing early

**Tax-free shopping after 1 November 2026:** NTA's announced refund method has eligible visitors pay tax-inclusive prices first, obtain customs confirmation that the goods are leaving Japan within 90 days of purchase, then receive the refund from the business. Carry enough funds for the gross purchase and check each retailer's refund terms. The 90 days is a customs-confirmation deadline, not a promise that a refund arrives within 90 days. [NTA April 2025 reform leaflet](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/shohi/menzei/202506/pdf/0025006-106.pdf) · [[Japan Travel Guide 2027#🧾 Tax-free shopping for the 2027 trip|Existing detailed shopping guidance]].

**International departure tax:** the tourism agency states **JPY 3,000 per departure from Japan from 1 July 2026**, generally collected through the ticket. Transitional treatment and exemptions exist. Check the ticket breakdown and count it once; the guide's international airfare sits outside the in-Japan envelope. This is separate from a shopping-tax refund. [Japan Tourism Agency departure-tax leaflet](https://www.mlit.go.jp/kankocho/content/001996466.pdf).

**Budget scenarios:** the blossom example uses **US$3,850 planned + US$1,150 reserve**. The seasonal route with Fuji-lakes transport uses **US$4,100 + US$900 reserve**. Both total US$5,000 excluding flights; their different transport/activity allowances are intentional scenarios. Use one model for the chosen route, then replace estimates with quotes. Resolve whether her US$200 gifts sit inside shopping before adding them. [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Seasonal budget and gift scenarios]].

## Wait for actual dates before completing these

- Compare flights using local departure/arrival dates, total fare, baggage and connection conditions. Preserve earlier searches as tests.
- Choose hotel check-in/out dates and occupancy, then quote full-stay totals and cancellation deadlines.
- Arrange the health consultation relative to actual departure, allowing time for any necessary follow-up.
- Complete Visit Japan Web using actual arrival and accommodation details. The Digital Agency describes immigration/customs preparation; this does not determine visa eligibility. [Digital Agency explanation](https://www.digital.go.jp/en/policies/visit_japan_web).
- Check annual attraction events, museum tickets, trail access, ferry services and transport booking windows; regenerate dated pocket cards for the chosen season.

## Keep research consistent as it grows

Use the official operator for inclusions and timetables, the government agency for rules, and destination guides for themes. A tour title, URL slug and search summary can disagree; read the selected package before drawing a conclusion. Repeated facts in the dashboard, essay and chapters share their underlying evidence and do not become independent verification.

For each new option, record the source/check date, actual destination and departure point, season, displayed currency and pricing unit, and unresolved package details. A quoted amount needs actual dates and travelers. When adding a destination, show which existing nights it replaces. Keep [[Japan 2027 — Bookings|the booking ledger]] for real confirmations.

**Next research choices:** choose two seasonal windows to compare, then develop one regional module and a comparable complete route budget for each. The existing [[Japan 2027 — Best Islands|island]], [[Japan 2027 — Sapporo Chapter|Sapporo]] and [[Japan 2027 — Fukuoka Chapter|Fukuoka]] notes offer starting points. The year, season, traveler count, passport and budget scope remain open.

**Source limits:** sources above were opened on 8 October 2026. JNTO/JMA month guidance is background climate/destination evidence. The Fuji page contains current-season dates, not verified 2027 access. NTA's shopping leaflet was published April 2025 and describes a future change effective 1 November 2026. The Digital Agency explanation is updated July 2024; its service landing page yielded no readable text, so no live form workflow was tested. Exact travel-date quotes, account setup and personal eligibility were not established.

# ✍️ Japan 2027 — Quick Capture

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|Dashboard]] · [[Japan 2027 — Today|Today]]

## Save something quickly

1. Reload Obsidian once after this setup so QuickAdd loads the new choices.
2. Open the command palette with **Ctrl+P**, search **Japan**, and choose a capture. You can also run **QuickAdd: Run** and select it there.
3. Fill in the form, then submit. Cancel to leave without saving. Enter `—` for an unused notes or photo field.

| Capture | Saves to | What to enter |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Japan — Expense | [[Japan 2027 — Expenses]] | Spending date, item, numeric amount, original currency, category, payment method, notes/receipt |
| Japan — Place | [[Japan 2027 — Place Inbox]] | Exact place or branch, area, source/Maps URL, why you want to visit |
| Japan — Memory | [[Japan 2027 — Memories#My entries]] | Memory date, place, moment, photo/clip filename or link |

Dates accept today or a specific date; check the saved date when recording an older entry. Amounts and URLs are text fields: check them before submitting. Captures append entries and do not update budgets, bookings, map pins, or the memory summary table automatically.

## Japanese review

Open [[Japan 2027 — Japanese Flashcards|your 22-card Japanese deck]] and use the Spaced Repetition flashcard review command. Keep the five-minute conversation practice in [[Japan 2027 — Japanese Practice]].

## Setup reference

The choices use QuickAdd's [capture formats](https://quickadd.obsidian.guide/docs/Choices/CaptureChoice/) and [one-page forms](https://quickadd.obsidian.guide/docs/Advanced/onePageInputs/). # 🌿 Japan 2027 — Sapporo chapter

**Klook tours to compare:** [[Japan 2027 — Klook More Cities and Islands#Sapporo and Otaru|Sapporo, Otaru, and Jozankei options]]

**Next chapter:** [[Japan 2027 — Nara Chapter|Nara]] — a Kyoto/Osaka day-trip alternative with temples, park, food, and crafts; separate from the Hokkaido module.

[[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|← Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — Sapporo Pocket Routes|Four pocket routes]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and bookings]]

[[#Google Maps — sights shopping and transport|📍 Google Maps]] · [[#Foods to try|🍜 Food]] · [[#Getting here — Haneda Narita and Kansai|🛬 Getting here]]

**Research checked:** 7 October 2026. **Working year:** 2027, unconfirmed. This chapter is an optional Hokkaido module. Dates, hotels, flights, restaurants, stock, and travel-day opening times are not confirmed. Recommendations below reflect your photography, food, clothes, hobby browsing, gifts, and outdoor interests.

## My starting recommendation

Start with **three nights**: arrival and a short central evening → Sapporo city day → Otaru day trip → departure. Choose **four nights** if you also want Jozankei hot springs or a separate Moerenuma photography day. Three nights give two full outing days, not three. Four nights are my preferred summer version if reducing another base feels worthwhile.

Sapporo is a Hokkaido base, substantially separate from your Kansai and Kyushu options. Compare it with [[Japan 2027 — Fukuoka Chapter|Fukuoka]] as another regional choice; do not automatically add both to the same 14 nights. Keep Fuji as its own scenic/summit decision.

## Which season suits your guide?

| Your option | What changes here | My recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Summer, especially July / early August** | Park walks, outdoor photography, and longer daylight; temperatures and rain still vary | Best first fit for this chapter. Compare four nights, while retaining breaks and a rain alternative. |
| **Second half of May** | Hokkaido is still in spring; lilacs are a better seasonal lead than assuming Tokyo/Kyoto warmth or cherry blossom timing | Good second choice for city food, Otaru, and spring greenery. Pack layers; check actual flowering. |
| **Mid / late November** | Your proposed Kyoto foliage trip occurs later than Sapporo's usual city foliage window | Choose Sapporo for food and a colder city visit; keep Kyoto time if autumn colors are the main goal. |
| **A future winter trip** | Snow experiences require a separate winter plan and current conditions | Snow Festival is normally early February; it does not belong to the summer/May/November itinerary. |

This ranking is my planning judgment. The [official summer guide](https://visit.sapporo.travel/seasons/summer/) describes summer outdoor options; the [spring guide](https://visit.sapporo.travel/seasons/spring/) places average cherry flowering around late April/early May and lilacs in May. Neither establishes 2027 peak dates or festival dates.

The [official autumn guide](https://visit.sapporo.travel/seasons/autumn/) gives **mid-October to early November** for city foliage. That is a general window, not a forecast or a guarantee that Jozankei and city trees peak together. The [winter guide](https://visit.sapporo.travel/seasons/winter/) describes winter roughly December–March, February's Snow Festival, and illuminations usually beginning in late November. Check future event announcements separately; November does not guarantee established ski conditions or festival snow scenes.

## Explore by area

| Area | Your reason to explore | Suggested use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Sapporo Station / Odori** | Arrival convenience, park and city architecture | Base near the station for rail days, or Odori for central walks; compare actual hotel quotes |
| **Tanukikoji / Susukino** | Covered shopping, collectibles, ramen, evening street photos | One shopping block and a selected dinner; no compulsory nightlife crawl |
| **Maruyama / Hokkaido Shrine** | Green spaces and shrine setting | City-day morning; park walking is the default outdoor activity |
| **Nakajima Park** | A quieter park-photo alternative | Substitute for another park when you want a slower day |
| **Hokkaido University** | Tree-lined campus and seasonal photography | Optional station-area walk on public visitor routes; respect teaching/research spaces |
| **Mt. Moiwa** | City panorama | Optional clear-weather ropeway evening, chosen instead of a long extra dinner/shopping circuit |
| **Otaru** | Canal, historic streets, sweets, craft browsing | A separate rail day trip |
| **Jozankei / Moerenuma** | Hot-spring relaxation OR landscape/art photography | Fourth-night extension: choose one outing |

Use the [city's first-visit walking itinerary](https://visit.sapporo.travel/itineraries/02/) for central orientation and the [official attraction directory](https://www.sapporo.travel/en/facility/) to select sights. The table is a suggested grouping, not a promise that every stop fits a single day. [Tanukikoji city listing](https://www.sapporo.travel/spot/facility/tanukikoji_shopping_arcade/).

### Outdoor views and photography

**Mt. Moiwa:** start by comparing a ropeway visit, rather than assign a summit hike without knowing your preferred effort. Check the [operator's current notices](https://mt-moiwa.jp/) and [access map](https://mt-moiwa.jp/access/map/) for the streetcar/shuttle/walking connection, operation, tickets, and final descent. A map pin at the lower station is not a complete ascent. Choose a clear forecast; keep dinner/shop plans flexible.

**Moerenuma Park:** Isamu Noguchi's landscape design, geometric landforms, and the Glass Pyramid make this my strongest optional architecture/photo outing. Reach the park using the operator's described **Toho subway to Kanjodori-Higashi → Chuo Bus to the appropriate park entrance** route, then verify the dated bus timetable and return. Entrance access and facilities change seasonally. Check the actual fountain schedule before planning a fountain shot; the current English page carries operating restrictions. [Park design, transport, and facilities](https://moerenumapark.jp/english/).

**Filming sequence:** a central street establishing shot → park/architecture detail → permitted food preparation or meal close-up → Otaru waterfront movement → city lights. Ask before recording inside shops, restaurants, shrine interiors, or museums. Moerenuma publishes photography/video requirements on its English page; consult those for a planned shoot. A small personal camera does not establish permission for every use.

## Foods to try

My first two priorities are **miso ramen** and **soup curry**, followed by **jingisukan** if grilled lamb appeals to you. Use Otaru for a seafood lunch and a sweet rather than schedule every specialty on arrival.

| Food | What to expect | Where I would put it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Sapporo miso ramen** | Ramen with a miso-based broth; toppings vary | Arrival evening or city lunch; Ramen Yokocho is one browsing lead |
| **Soup curry** | Spiced broth with substantial vegetables and optional meat | City-day lunch; choose spice level and ingredients from the actual menu |
| **Jingisukan** | Grilled lamb/mutton | One dinner; compare portions, cooking format, and price before committing |
| **Seafood / kaisendon** | Fish or seafood over rice; ingredients vary | Nijo Market or Otaru lunch after looking at the displayed menu |
| **Sweets and a café break** | Choose a pastry, chocolate, or local dessert you actually want | Otaru sweet stop or Shiroi Koibito Park as an alternative outing |

[Official city food portal](https://quruquru.sapporo.travel/en/list/?id=2%2F) · [City explanation of ramen and soup curry](https://visit.sapporo.travel/seasons/winter/) · [Nijo and Curb Market visitor guide](https://www.sapporo.travel/en/spot/feature/sapporo-nijo-market-curb-market/). Seafood prices and availability vary; this is not a quoted crab/uni meal or a restaurant reservation.

**Sapporo Beer Museum / Beer Garden:** optional history and a separate meal/tasting decision. The museum and nearby restaurant are not one automatic booking, and the actual brewery in Eniwa is a different location. Older guides describe free self-guided admission; the [current Japanese visitor page](https://www.sapporobeer.jp/brewery/s_museum/kengaku/) lists paid admission. Use the [current English operator page](https://www.sapporobeer.jp/english/brewery/s_museum/) and dated booking information before budgeting; do not rely on an old free-entry assumption. No alcohol or museum visit is required for this route.

For an izakaya evening, use **Susukino as an area lead**, then pick a menu, seating arrangement, and total price closer to travel. It is not a verified individual-restaurant shortlist or a new ranking against Osaka/Tokyo.

## Shopping, perfume browsing, and gifts

| Lead | What to browse | Current source / limitation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Tanukikoji arcade** | A compact clothing/accessory or practical souvenir browse | [Official city listing](https://www.sapporo.travel/spot/facility/tanukikoji_shopping_arcade/). Area lead; no exact vintage inventory or size confirmed. |
| **Mandarake Sapporo** | Used toys, collectibles, models, discs, and games | **nORBESA 2F, 1-1 Minami 3-jo Nishi 5-chome, Chuo-ku.** [Official branch](https://www.mandarake.co.jp/dir/spr/). Inspect condition and actual stock; no Tamiya item promised. |
| **Sapporo PARCO** | Tees, shirts, trousers, denim or an accessory; compare current tenants | [Official shop list](https://sapporo.parco.jp/shop/?category_search=category). For her, look for Y2K/Korean-inspired pieces she would enjoy; no 2027 trend forecast or size guarantee. |
| **NOSE SHOP at PARCO** | Fragrance testing as a lead for her gift | Listed in the [current first-floor guide](https://sapporo.parco.jp/floor/detail/?f=1f&ft=type1). Confirm branch/brands, available testers, and small formats closer to travel. |
| **Shiroi Koibito Park** | Sweets, shop browsing, café, optional paid experiences | [Operator's English guide](https://www.shiroikoibitopark.jp/en/). An alternative half-day activity; do not force it into the city/Otaru core. |
| **Otaru craft-shopping streets** | One small craft or decorative gift after seeing it in person | [Otaru tourist association](https://otaru.gr.jp/). Treat shop pins and inventories as separate checks. |

Your **L tops / EU 44–46 trousers** are starting references; try on or compare garment measurements. Her size remains unknown, so a small accessory is easier to choose than fitted clothes. Fragrance preferences remain unknown too: test first and compare a small format if offered; a fashion preference does not establish a scent preference. Your Tokyo Tamiya visit remains the specific Tamiya anchor.

### Souvenir shortlist

1. **For her: Shiroi Koibito or another clearly labeled packaged sweet.** Compare a small box at the park/shop, with ingredients, expiry, and storage checked. [Park's official shop overview](https://www.shiroikoibitopark.jp/en/).
2. **For you or her: ROYCE' Pure Chocolate or a packaged alternative.** The [manufacturer's product guide](https://www.royce.com/contents/english_product/) distinguishes storage needs. Pure Chocolate is listed at **25°C or below**; it still needs protection from summer heat. **Nama Chocolate requires 10°C or below**, so choose it only with a workable cold-storage/transport plan. Some varieties contain liquor; choose by the label.
3. **Enjoy LeTAO Double Fromage locally instead of assuming it is a suitcase gift.** The [manufacturer's product page](https://www.letao.jp/category/DOUBLE_FROMAGE/F011.html) lists frozen storage. Check café servings in Otaru; for travel, compare another product whose printed storage instructions suit your journey.
4. **One compact accessory or collectible.** Choose by personal appeal, condition, weight, and price. A new city does not create another shopping allowance.

Her choices remain within the existing **US$200 gift cap**, with the relationship to your **US$300 shopping/miscellaneous allowance** still open. If included in that US$300, up to US$100 remains for your other shopping. If additional within the US$5,000 envelope, the existing planning scenario is US$4,300 planned / US$700 reserve. Count a perfume, sweets, or fashion purchase once. [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her|Gift plan]] · [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist|Personal shortlist]].

## Otaru day trip

**Route:** Sapporo Station → a dated JR service to **Otaru Station** → walk the central area → JR back to Sapporo. Confirm whether your service is direct or needs a change. Use [JR Hokkaido's Sapporo/Otaru service information](https://www.jrhokkaido.co.jp/global/english/travel/airport.html) and the [Otaru tourist association](https://otaru.gr.jp/) for updated visitor information.

My day: **canal and warehouse-area photos → seafood lunch → Sakaimachi-area browsing → one café/sweet → return**. The canal and Sakaimachi shopping area are separate walking blocks; cut shopping if your pace or weather requires. Otaru's historic harbor/warehouse character is described by [Hokkaido's official tourism guide](https://en.visit-hokkaido.jp/adventure-travel/traveltrade-press/destinations/otaru/).

Blue-hour canal photos are optional. Check that the actual return train, shop closures, and dinner plan support a later stay. No canal cruise, glass workshop, music-box purchase, or specific café seat has been booked.

## Jozankei hot springs — fourth-night choice

**Route:** central Sapporo → **Jotetsu Kappa Liner** or another confirmed bus → your selected Jozankei stop → short town/river walk and a selected day-use bath → bus back. Compare the [tourist association's access page](https://jozankei.jp/en/access/) with the [operator's multilingual transport page](https://www.jotetsu.co.jp/bus/global/index.html) for the actual boarding point, timetable, booking requirements, and return. Reserve the Kappa Liner when required; do not substitute an old bus-stop screenshot for a confirmed seat.

Choose the bathing facility first: check day-use admission, last entry, closures, towels, and house rules, then coordinate the bus. Use the [association's hot-spring listings](https://jozankei.jp/en/) to compare options. River walks depend on weather/access. Autumn viewpoints and seasonal shuttles need a dated check; the [official foliage program](https://jozankei.jp/en/godaikoyo/) is a planning lead, not confirmation of 2027 bus operation or peak colors.

This is a separate outing from Otaru. A Jozankei overnight would replace a Sapporo hotel night and change luggage/transfers; it has not been added. Furano/Biei, Hakodate, and Niseko would need their own wider Hokkaido route rather than extra compulsory day trips in this short chapter.

## Getting here — Haneda, Narita, and Kansai

**Default airport: New Chitose (CTS).** Search the exact airport code: Sapporo's **Okadama (OKD)** is a different airport and is not the arrival used in this chapter.

| Starting point | Route to compare | Before choosing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Haneda (HND)** | Domestic flight HND → CTS → airport rail to Sapporo → hotel | Same-airport terminal transfer, actual connection time, baggage rules, and check-in deadline |
| **Narita (NRT)** | Compare a direct NRT → CTS flight on your date → rail to Sapporo | Availability, airline/terminal, fare with baggage, and international-to-domestic connection rules; do not assume a transfer to HND is needed |
| **Kyoto / Osaka** | Hotel → selected Kansai airport, such as ITM or KIX → CTS → Sapporo | Compare the full hotel-to-hotel journey; ITM and KIX are different airports |
| **International arrival directly at CTS** | Clear arrival formalities → follow signs toward domestic-terminal airport rail station → Sapporo | Correct terminal walking route and late-arrival train options |
| **Sapporo back to Tokyo** | Sapporo → CTS → selected Tokyo airport → final Tokyo hotel | Retain the planned Tokyo buffer nights; do not attach a day trip before the flight |

Use [JAL's domestic route map/timetables](https://www.jal.co.jp/jp/en/dom/route/index.html) and [Peach's route map](https://www.flypeach.com/en/lm/st/routemap) to compare dated service. These are research tools, not held flights or confirmed 2027 schedules. Avoid a same-day cross-airport transfer without accounting for the entire ground journey and connection rules.

**CTS → city:** [JR Hokkaido's airport guide](https://www.jrhokkaido.co.jp/global/english/travel/airport.html) describes Airport train services to Sapporo and Otaru; select the actual train/destination. The station is reached from the domestic terminal basement, including a terminal walk when arriving internationally. [JR airport terminal map](https://www.jrhokkaido.co.jp/global/pdf/e-chitose.pdf). Do not equate flight landing time with reaching the station platform. Within the city, compare subway/walking connections to your hotel; station-area and Odori bases serve different priorities.

## Fit it into 15 days / 14 nights

These are **alternatives**, not additions to the existing dated cherry-blossom cards or a commitment to three separate trips.

| Alternative | Nights | Tradeoff |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Three-night Sapporo module** | Tokyo 4 → Kawaguchiko 2 → Kyoto 3 → Sapporo 3 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | City + Otaru; replaces three Kyoto nights in the default seasonal outline. No separate Jozankei day. |
| **Four-night summer preference** | Tokyo 4 → Kawaguchiko 2 → Kyoto 2 → Sapporo 4 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Adds Jozankei OR Moerenuma, but Kyoto gets only one full non-transfer day. Choose knowingly. |
| **Hokkaido-focused alternative** | Tokyo 4 → Kawaguchiko 2 → Sapporo 6 → Tokyo 2 = **14** | Omits Kyoto/Osaka; permits slower Sapporo and separate day trips. A wider regional circuit still needs fresh routing. |

All versions retain your scenic Fuji option, not an automatic summit climb. For November foliage, my starting choice remains more Kyoto time rather than the four-night Sapporo route. Requote domestic flights, airport ground travel, rail/bus day trips, hotels, and activities within the **US$5,000 in-Japan ceiling**. The US$400 intercity planning allocation is not evidence these flights fit. Compare total trip cost before selecting a route. No new city shopping allowance or hotel quote has been introduced.

# 🌿 Sapporo — pocket routes

[[Japan 2027 — Sapporo Chapter|← Full chapter, sources, transport, and Google Maps]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget / reservations]]

**Undated cards:** arrival + two full outing days + departure for three nights. Use Cards 1–3, adapting arrival to the actual flight. Add Card 4 only with a fourth night; choose its A or B outing. These cards do not change the 15 dated spring cards or establish shop opening times.

## Card 1 — arrive, settle in, first Sapporo meal

**Anchor:** CTS → JR airport train → Sapporo Station → selected hotel, or the equivalent confirmed city connection.

| Block | Plan |
| --- | --- |
| Arrival | Correct terminal → baggage/arrival formalities → airport rail → hotel. Confirm luggage/check-in arrangements. |
| If daylight and energy allow | Short Odori or hotel-area walk; do not attempt the whole sightseeing circuit. |
| Dinner | Miso ramen or another nearby meal; Ramen Yokocho only if convenient and open. |
| Optional evening | A short Susukino street-photo walk with a planned return. |

**Food:** one ramen meal. **Shot:** arrival transition → city grid → food close-up with permission. **Buy:** none required. **Late-arrival fallback:** hotel-area dinner and rest.

- [ ] CTS airport code, terminal, final usable train, and hotel connection checked.
- [ ] Tomorrow's weather and city block chosen.

## Card 2 — green Sapporo, soup curry, and shopping

**Anchor:** Maruyama / shrine → central lunch → Tanukikoji / one shop → chosen evening.

| Block | Plan |
| --- | --- |
| Morning | Maruyama Park and permitted Hokkaido Shrine areas; keep it a manageable park walk. |
| Lunch | Soup curry; choose ingredients and spice level. |
| Afternoon | Tanukikoji plus Mandarake OR PARCO. Choose a priority rather than force every shop. |
| Evening | Jingisukan / izakaya-style dinner OR a clear-weather Mt. Moiwa ropeway visit followed by a simpler meal. |

**Shot:** green canopy → shrine exterior → texture/collectible with permission → city lights. **Buy:** one garment/accessory OR collectible within the existing cap. If comparing NOSE SHOP perfumes, test before considering a small format; her preferences remain open.

**Rain alternative:** selected central shopping and a café; Beer Museum only after checking admission/opening. **May adjustment:** layers and actual flower conditions. **November adjustment:** shorter outdoor blocks and actual daylight; no foliage guarantee. **Summer adjustment:** hydration, seated breaks, and shade when needed.

- [ ] Selected shops and dinner menu/price checked; actual measurements before buying clothes.
- [ ] Ropeway operations, access, final descent, and weather checked if chosen.
- [ ] Ask before indoor filming; do not automatically film other diners.

## Card 3 — Otaru, canal photos, seafood, and one sweet

**Route:** Sapporo Station → dated JR train to Otaru Station → central walking blocks → JR return. Confirm direct service or transfer, and final return suitable for your evening.

| Block | Plan |
| --- | --- |
| Morning | Canal / warehouse-area walk and photos. |
| Lunch | Seafood or another suitable meal after checking the menu. |
| Afternoon | Sakaimachi-area craft browsing, then one café/sweet. |
| Return | Train back to Sapporo; a relaxed dinner near the hotel. |

**Shot:** canal wide → warehouse detail → street movement → dessert. **Buy:** one compact craft OR packaged sweet; do not assume a frozen cheesecake can travel unrefrigerated. **Blue hour:** optional only if return transport, weather, and shop closures fit. **Fallback:** shorten walking and use cafés/shops; change to an indoor city day if conditions make the outing unsuitable.

- [ ] Rail route/return and a realistic walking loop checked.
- [ ] Ingredients, expiry, and storage reviewed for edible gifts.
- [ ] No cruise, workshop, café reservation, or craft purchase assumed.

## Card 4 — fourth-night extension: choose A OR B

### A. Jozankei — hot springs and a short river walk

**Route:** selected city boarding point → reserved Kappa Liner when required or a verified alternative bus → correct Jozankei stop → return bus. Confirm both directions before departure.

**Main block:** a short town/river walk → lunch → preselected day-use bath → return. Choose the bathing facility and check access, hours, last entry, towels, and rules before settling the bus plan. No Jozankei overnight is counted.

**Shot:** town/river exterior details; no bath photography. **Buy:** optional compact souvenir only. **Fallback:** an indoor Sapporo day if transport, access, or weather does not work. Autumn-color scenes are conditional, especially in November.

### B. Moerenuma — landscape and architecture

**Route lead:** Toho subway → Kanjodori-Higashi → dated Chuo Bus to the appropriate Moerenuma entrance → return. Consult the park's current access information; check entrance changes and the last workable return.

**Main block:** manageable park loop → geometric landscape / Glass Pyramid if open → break → city return. Check fountain operations before assigning a fountain shot; its schedule is seasonal and can carry restrictions.

**Shot:** wide landform → geometric detail → walking transition → permitted pyramid interior. **Buy:** none required. **Fallback:** central architecture/café/shopping when wind, rain, facility closure, or bus service limits the outing.

- [ ] Choose A or B; do not schedule both with Otaru on one day.
- [ ] Dated transport, facility opening, and total outing cost checked.
- [ ] Planned filming fits facility rules.

**Departure:** hotel → CTS → onward flight, with baggage/check-in buffers. Keep excursions off the flight day. The Tokyo-return route separately retains its two Tokyo nights before international departure.

# 🗺️ Japan 2027 — Saved map files and offline setup

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|← Dashboard]] · [[Japan 2027 — Maps|All Google Maps links]] · [[Japan 2027 — Map View|Interactive starter maps]]

**Saved 6 October 2026.** These files contain your guide’s places and pins. They are not downloaded Google Maps offline areas. No Google account import or iPhone download has been performed.

## Your saved map files

- [[Attachments/Japan 2027 Maps/Japan 2027 — All places.csv|All places CSV]] — 186 distinct search queries, including shops, cafés, transport anchors, and sightseeing ideas. Some rows are chain searches or alternate queries for the same place. They are not 186 independently verified venues.
- [[Attachments/Japan 2027 Maps/Japan 2027 — Starter pins.kml|Starter pins KML]] — 11 sourced location records for Tokyo, Kyoto, and optional Uji. Coordinates identify sites, not confirmed entrances or station platforms.

## Download Google Maps areas on your iPhone

On Wi-Fi, open **Google Maps → profile picture → Offline maps → Select your own map**. Adjust the rectangle and tap **Download**. Repeat for each area below, where downloads are available. Downloaded areas stay inside the Google Maps app.

- [ ] **Tokyo:** include your booked hotel, Asakusa/Ueno, Ginza/Tokyo Station, Shinjuku, Shibuya, and the western neighborhoods you choose to visit. Download overlapping areas if one selection cannot include everything.
- [ ] **Airport coverage:** include Haneda or Narita and the surrounding area after confirming your flights. Narita is far from central Tokyo; check its coverage separately.
- [ ] **Kyoto:** include your booked hotel, Kyoto Station, central/eastern Kyoto, and Arashiyama if selected. Add Ohara separately if you choose that outing.
- [ ] **Optional excursion:** add Uji, Kamakura, Nara, or Osaka only for the destinations you actually select.
- [ ] Turn on **Auto-update offline maps** in Offline maps settings. Recheck downloads and expiry dates during the week before your March 2027 departure.
- [ ] Test downloaded coverage in airplane mode and save screenshots of important station exits and the airport/hotel journeys while online.

**Offline limitation:** Google Maps does not provide walking, public-transport, or cycling directions offline. Keep your eSIM available for transit planning and store the important route details separately. Offline areas are separate from saved-place lists and these CSV/KML exports.

[Google’s official iPhone download instructions](https://support.google.com/maps/answer/6291838?co=GENIE.Platform%3DiOS&hl=en)

## Import the files into a custom Google My Maps map

On your computer, sign in to [Google My Maps](https://www.google.com/mymaps), create a map called **Japan 2027**, then **Add layer → Import**.

1. Import the KML for the 11 sourced pins.
2. In a separate layer, import the CSV. Select **Place query** as the location column and **Name** as the title column.
3. Review Google’s matches and correct ambiguous branches, broad chain searches, and duplicate destinations before relying on the result.

Importing places into My Maps does not download an offline Google Maps area. [Google’s import instructions](https://support.google.com/mymaps/answer/3024836?hl=en)

## Checks at the useful time

1. **Before setting dates:** choose year/season, hiking level, Fuji goal, traveler count, and airports; compare full route quotes.
2. **Before each booking:** verify the dated official timetable/access notice, exact price, luggage/inclusions, cancellation terms, and running total.
3. **When annual announcements appear:** update festival arrangements, Fuji openings/fees/reservation rules, autumn illuminations, and relevant transport booking windows. Current 2026 details are not 2027 confirmation.
4. **Close to departure:** recheck entry/transit requirements for your actual nationality/route, attraction/trail access, and local conditions; use [[Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials|the existing essentials]] as a checklist reference and refresh date-sensitive facts.
5. **Once dates are chosen:** create seasonal dated pocket cards, actual station-to-hotel routes, and calendar-specific opening checks. Do not reuse the blossom cards' dates.
6. **Each evening:** back up photos, record spending, and settle next day's main stop and fallback.

# 🛍️ Japan 2027 — Tokyo neighborhoods for shops and cafés

**Kichijoji:** [[Japan 2027 — Kichijoji Shopping and Exploring|What to buy, explore, and photograph — optional half-day]]

**Shopping shortlist:** [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist|🛍️ Priorities, Tamiya, and spending caps]]

**Maps:** [[Japan 2027 — Maps|📍 All destination, shop, café, and transport searches]]

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|← Start Here]] · [[Japan Travel Guide 2027|Full guide]] · [[Japan 2027 — Hidden Gems|Slower discoveries]]

**Checked 6 October 2026.** Tokyo is a prefecture; these are neighborhoods within it. Kichijoji is in Musashino City, while the others below are within Tokyo’s special wards. These are optional outings, with planning allowances excluding travel. Recheck shop locations, opening days, menus, and access before March–April 2027. No tax-free service is assumed from a shop’s inclusion here.

| Neighborhood | Best fit | Concrete starting points | Suggested time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Koenji, Suginami** | Vintage clothing and independent browsing | SAFARI branches; Pal Shopping Street | 3–4 hours |
| **Shimokitazawa, Setagaya** | Vintage shops plus a coffee break | New York Joe Exchange; existing 2nd STREET option; LIGHT UP COFFEE | 3–4 hours |
| **Kichijoji, Musashino** | Vintage, specialty coffee, and a park outing | SAFARI; LIGHT UP COFFEE; Inokashira Park | Half-day |
| **Daikanyama, Shibuya** | Books, design browsing, and a relaxed café | TSUTAYA BOOKS / T-SITE; IVY PLACE | 2–3 hours |
| **Nakameguro, Meguro** | Travel stationery and neighborhood photography | TRAVELER’S FACTORY; existing Meguro River outing | 1–2 hours for shopping, separate from photography |
| **Kuramae, Taito** | Stationery, writing tools, and chocolate café | Kakimori; Dandelion Chocolate Factory & Café | 2–3 hours |

## 👖 Koenji — my first new pick for vintage

Start with a short section of **Pal Shopping Street**, then choose one or two SAFARI branches rather than touring them all. SAFARI’s official directory separates vintage clothing, Ralph Lauren/RRL, outdoor clothing, and European vintage across its Koenji stores. Choose the branch by your style. Selected or collectible vintage can cost more than ordinary secondhand clothing; compare condition and fit before buying.

**Route fit:** an alternative Tokyo half-day on 30 March or 7 April. Koenji and Kichijoji lie along the JR Chuo corridor, but each merits its own browsing time. Pick one if you want a relaxed outing and check which train stops at your station on the travel date.

[SAFARI official store directory](https://e-safari.co.jp/) · [Official Koenji shopping guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/book/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/AO_2603_suginamichuo_low_EN.pdf) · [Maps: Koenji Pal Shopping Street](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Koenji+Pal+Shopping+Street+Tokyo)

## ☕ Shimokitazawa — the easiest vintage-and-coffee combination

The guide already includes Shimokitazawa. GO TOKYO identifies **New York Joe Exchange** for secondhand clothing and **Reload** for shops and cafés. Browse after lunch, then consider LIGHT UP COFFEE’s Shimokitazawa branch at **2-29-12 Daita**. The coffee shop sits on the Setagaya-Daita side, so check the walk rather than assuming it is beside your clothing store.

**Route fit:** substitute for another optional shopping session. Keep one main vintage purchase in mind; do not increase the shopping budget to fit extra shops.

[Official neighborhood guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/destinations/western-tokyo/shimokitazawa/index.html) · [LIGHT UP COFFEE branches](https://lightupcoffee.com/pages/store) · [Maps: New York Joe Shimokitazawa](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=New+York+Joe+Exchange+Shimokitazawa+Tokyo) · [Maps: coffee branch](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=LIGHT+UP+COFFEE+Shimokitazawa+2-29-12+Daita)

## 🌳 Kichijoji — shops, coffee, and greenery

SAFARI lists **Nanaibashi** and **Nakamichi-dori** shops for American vintage. LIGHT UP COFFEE’s Kichijoji branch is at **4-13-15 Kichijoji Honmachi**, and its official Japanese page lists a three-coffee tasting option. Combine selected shopping with a coffee stop and the existing Inokashira Park option; plot the entrances before deciding the order.

**Route fit:** develop the guide’s existing Kichijoji alternative on 30 March or 7 April. Return with time to pack. Café English/Japanese pages currently differ on hours and prices, so use current local notices before visiting; no fixed 2027 price is assumed.

[SAFARI branches](https://e-safari.co.jp/) · [Coffee shop and menu](https://lightupcoffee.com/pages/store) · [Official Kichijoji guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/destinations/western-tokyo/kichijoji/) · [Maps: coffee](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=LIGHT+UP+COFFEE+Kichijoji+Tokyo)

## 📚 Daikanyama — books, design, and a polished café stop

Browse **DAIKANYAMA TSUTAYA BOOKS / T-SITE**, then consider **IVY PLACE**, a café/bar/dining venue at the complex. This is my pick when you want attractive surroundings and books rather than a clothing hunt. Check the current menu, reservation options, and prices before choosing a meal.

**Route fit:** the existing 29 March free block before Nakameguro. Drop this addition if blossom conditions make another photo location the priority.

[Official neighborhood guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/destinations/western-tokyo/daikanyama/index.html) · [IVY PLACE official listing](https://store.tsite.jp/daikanyama/floor/shop/ivy-place/) · [Maps: T-SITE](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Daikanyama+T-SITE+Tokyo)

## 📓 Nakameguro — a souvenir for your travel journal

**TRAVELER’S FACTORY Nakameguro**, at **3-13-10 Kamimeguro**, sells notebooks, customization parts, and related travel goods. The brand describes a second-floor space for books, customization, and coffee; check current availability before counting on seating. Its current closure day is Tuesday, so reconfirm before visiting.

**Route fit:** your existing Nakameguro day on Monday 29 March. Keep the shopping stop short enough to protect the river photo block. Cherry-blossom crowds can affect the whole outing.

[Official flagship information](https://www.travelers-company.com/about) · [Current store notices](https://www.travelers-factory.com/) · [Maps: flagship](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=TRAVELERS+FACTORY+Nakameguro+Tokyo)

## ✒️ Kuramae — stationery and chocolate

**Kakimori** is the writing-focused shop to consider: notebooks, pens, inks, and papers, including order-made notebooks. If customization is your priority, confirm turnaround and any booking requirement before going. **Dandelion Chocolate Factory & Café Kuramae** makes a separate café stop for chocolate drinks or sweets; check its current menu.

**Route fit:** pair with an Asakusa outing only if that day has space, or replace another optional Tokyo half-day. This is a stationery-and-café choice rather than a main thrift-shopping destination.

[Kakimori official shop](https://kakimori.com/en/pages/kakimori) · [Dandelion official café](https://www.dandelionchocolate-jp.com/pages/factory-cafe-kuramae) · [Maps: Kakimori](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Kakimori+Kuramae+Tokyo) · [Maps: Dandelion](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Dandelion+Chocolate+Factory+Cafe+Kuramae+Tokyo)

Vintage here describes the merchandise and browsing experience; modern retail interiors may differ. Reconfirm addresses and opening days for 2027. Tax-free participation has not been verified for these additions.

### ☕ A record-listening café

**RECOCO Shimokitazawa** offers a hands-on vinyl-listening café experience at **2-9-2 Kitazawa, ARISTO Shimokitazawa B1F**. It is a café stop; its site does not establish it as a used-record shop. Current rules include an entry charge plus one drink, cashless payment, no seat reservations, and a 90-minute limit when busy. Check the current menu and fees before going. Do not bring your newly purchased records in for listening: its published rules prohibit outside records.

[Official café and rules](https://recoco.cafe/) · [Maps: Shimokitazawa branch](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=RECOCO+Shimokitazawa+Tokyo)

### 🧭 Three optional outings

- **Music + café + thrift:** Shimokitazawa, with Disk Union and either RECOCO or the previously listed LIGHT UP COFFEE. Allow about 3–4 hours on site as a planning estimate; select a few stops.
- **Records + movies + park:** Kichijoji, choosing COCONUTS DISK and/or Disk Union alongside the existing park/coffee option. This is a half-day alternative for 30 March or 7 April, with time to pack afterwards.
- **Vintage film memorabilia:** Nakano Broadway, with Katsudo Shashin-kan as the anchor. Allow 2–3 hours on site as a planning estimate. Nakano is along the Chuo corridor like Koenji and Kichijoji, but adding all three would reduce browsing time; choose the main priority.

Shibuya, Shinjuku, and Ikebukuro shops can fit an outing already in that neighborhood. Recheck the exact entrances and floors, and use a current transit route. See [Mandarake’s current Nakano store guide](https://www.mandarake.co.jp/dir/nkn/index-en.html) for the broader complex.

### 💿 Before buying

- **Vinyl:** ask to inspect the disc and sleeve, understand the shop’s grading, and check missing inserts or an obi strip if completeness matters. Ask whether preview listening is available. Keep records supported and protected from heat or bending in luggage.
- **Movies:** check the exact edition’s region code, audio tracks, and subtitles against your player before paying. Do not assume Japanese releases have English or Spanish subtitles, or that a collector’s edition works on your home player.
- **Posters/scripts:** ask whether the item is original or a reproduction, inspect folds and wear, and request suitable protective packaging. Do not fold a poster simply to fit your suitcase.
- **Budget:** music and film souvenirs share the existing US$300 shopping/miscellaneous allowance. Pick a personal spending cap before browsing; collectible originals can be expensive.

## ⭐ What I would choose

- **Main vintage session:** Koenji or the already-planned Shimokitazawa option.
- **A balanced half-day:** Kichijoji, with one café and selected shops.
- **An easy existing-route addition:** Daikanyama or TRAVELER’S FACTORY before/around Nakameguro.
- **Stationery and gifts:** Kuramae.

Keep meals and cafés within the existing food allowance and purchases within the shopping allowance. Map links are searches; verify exact branches. Ask staff before filming inside independent shops or cafés.

## 🛒 Donki practical shopping stop

[[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist#Don Quijote — snacks, small gifts, and practical buys|Donki buying ideas and visit checklist]] — Ginza Honkan is a proposed short extension to the Shinbashi/Tamiya day. Product links, branch details, stock limits, and a basket cap are in the shortlist.

Choose one scenario before comparing totals. This assumes one traveler, 14 nights, and up to 15 calendar days; it is an estimate, not a quote.

- **Accommodation target:** US$2,100 total, averaging US$150 per night including applicable taxes and fees.
- **Meals/drinks target:** US$600 across 15 days.
- **Other planned costs:** US$1,150 for rail, local/airport transport, experiences, connectivity/insurance, shopping, and miscellaneous.
- **Separate costs:** international flights and the guide’s separate equipment purchases.

# 🗻 Summer Fuji — optional summit branch

[[Japan 2027 — Summer Guide|← Summer guide]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and booking records]]

**Decision still open:** you requested hiking and a Fuji visit; a summit climb has not been selected. Your hiking experience and preferred effort are not recorded. Scenic lake/viewpoint visits remain the default summer route.

The [official climbing site](https://www.fujisan-climb.jp/en/) publishes trail openings, reservations, fees, preparation, and notices. Summer opening differs by trail and year; **2026 information is background, not confirmation of the 2027 season**. Do not apply old fee, gate-hour, or booking-opening details to a future purchase.

## How the climb changes the itinerary

Compare this **14-night alternative** only if you choose the summit:

**Tokyo 4 → Kawaguchiko 1 → mountain hut 1 → Kawaguchiko 2 → Kyoto 4 → Tokyo 2 = 14 nights.**

| Day | Proposed block | Night |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1–4 | Same Tokyo block as the summer guide | Tokyo 4 |
| 5 | Transfer to Kawaguchiko; organize equipment and next day's verified access | Kawaguchiko |
| 6 | Travel to the chosen trailhead, acclimatize, climb to a reserved hut | Mountain hut |
| 7 | Optional summit attempt if appropriate, descend on the correct trail, return and rest | Kawaguchiko |
| 8 | Recovery / local lake day; no long intercity transfer planned | Kawaguchiko |
| 9 | Transfer to Kyoto on a verified connection | Kyoto |
| 10–12 | Kyoto highlights, reduced to fit the climb branch | Kyoto 3 further nights |
| 13–14 | Tokyo return, shopping, packing | Tokyo 2 |
| 15 | Departure | — |

**Day 8 is a recovery day.** If a weather delay shifts the climb, replace further Kyoto time or extend the trip explicitly before changing reservations. Buffer time does not guarantee a delayed hut or transport reservation can be moved.

The hut replaces a hotel night; it is not an extra uncounted night. Luggage storage/forwarding and transport after descent require confirmation. A sunrise summit is optional, not a reason to climb through the night without rest. The official [Yoshida model plan](https://www.fujisan-climb.jp/en/modelplan-yoshida/) provides a starting reference for a hut-based climb; a guide/operator should settle the route, pace, and suitability if selected.

## What to verify before selecting this branch

- [ ] Prior mountain experience, usual walking hours, and readiness for a strenuous high-altitude outing.
- [ ] Exact trail, opening period, current access rules, admission/reservation requirements, and current fee.
- [ ] Mountain hut: confirmed night, location on the selected trail, meals, bedding, arrival requirements, cancellation terms.
- [ ] Guide/operator if wanted: actual quote, inclusions, equipment, weather/turnaround policy, and meeting point.
- [ ] Proper clothing/footwear, waterproofs, headlamp, food/water, and the official preparation guidance; summer city clothing alone is insufficient.
- [ ] Trailhead transport both ways, descent route, return accommodation, and itinerary recovery time.
- [ ] Weather/trail notices and an agreed turnaround plan; the summit remains optional.
- [ ] Actual total for hut, access, transport, guide, and equipment, with reductions elsewhere if required to stay inside the cap.

**Cost status:** no hut, guide, equipment-rental, or admission quote. No summit reservation has been made. Do not add these costs invisibly to the scenic-route budget. If you choose scenic Fuji, retain the summer guide's two lake nights and skip this branch.

**Osaka option:** [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Chapter]] provides a Kyoto day trip or two-night variation, replacing Kyoto time while preserving 14 nights; summer heat breaks are included. [[Japan 2027 — Osaka Pocket Routes|Open the pocket routes]]. The daily table below remains the original scenic-Fuji concept.

[[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Guides|← Seasonal guides]] · [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|Budget and reservations]] · [[Japan 2027 — Autumn Guide|Autumn alternative]]

**Status:** planning guide; no dates, hotels, transport, festival tickets, or climbs booked. **Working length:** 15 days / 14 nights, one traveler as a budgeting assumption. **Your interests:** photography/filming, hiking, Fuji, cafés and matcha, vintage clothes, Tamiya, records, and gifts.

## Best starting window

My first window to compare is **late July into early August**, before the main mid-August Obon travel period. Early July can overlap the rainy season; July/August city sightseeing requires planning for heat and humidity. This is a seasonal recommendation, not a forecast or a promise of lower prices. [JNTO July](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/july/) · [JNTO August](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/august/).

If Gion Matsuri is a priority, choose the Kyoto nights around the festival rather than preserving the order below. Kyoto's official guide identifies **July 17 and 24** as procession highlights within the July festival; the exact **2027 arrangements, access, and paid seats need checking**. [Kyoto festival information](https://global.kyoto.travel/en/faq/detail.php?faq_id=2025). No firework date or seasonal event has been assigned to this itinerary.

## Route and accommodation count

**Tokyo 4 nights → Kawaguchiko 2 → Kyoto 6 → Tokyo 2 = 14 nights.** This scenic-Fuji route is the default. Choose air-conditioned rooms with practical station access and cancellable terms where possible; lake-view lodging is an optional upgrade, not a requirement. Compare a Tokyo return flight against departing Osaka/KIX before fixing the last two nights.

Tokyo → Kawaguchiko: compare [direct highway bus](https://bus.fujikyu.co.jp/en/lp/en-shinjuku-kawaguchiko/) and [FUJI EXCURSION rail](https://e.fujikyu-railway.jp/fujikaiyuu/). Kawaguchiko → Kyoto: check the [Mishima–Kawaguchiko liner](https://bus.fujikyu.co.jp/en/highway/mishima/) in the required direction, then the [Tokaido Shinkansen](https://global.jr-central.co.jp/en/). Allow a connection buffer for road delays. If that dated connection is unsuitable, compare returning through Tokyo before Kyoto. Fares and schedules are unconfirmed.

## Fifteen-day outline

These are my proposed outing blocks, not attraction opening times. Choose one main outdoor block per day and use lunch/cafés/indoor browsing to break up the heat. Swap adjacent days when weather or opening notices justify it.

| Day / night | Main plan | Flexible or bad-weather alternative | Photo/film prompt |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 / Tokyo 1 | Arrive, hotel transfer, food, rest | Nearby indoor meal | First neighborhood scene |
| 2 / Tokyo 2 | Asakusa early; café and indoor browsing later | Shorten walking and stay near transport | Temple detail / river scene |
| 3 / Tokyo 3 | Kichijoji: choose park, vintage, coffee, and one record shop | LOFT or record browsing with breaks | Pond reflection / shop entrance |
| 4 / Tokyo 4 | Choose an early Takao walk if conditions suit; otherwise a Tokyo park | Shops/café day; full hike is optional | Forest/temple detail |
| 5 / Kawaguchiko 1 | Travel to the Fuji lakes; hotel and lakeside walk | Café and local orientation | Wide lake-and-mountain view if visible |
| 6 / Kawaguchiko 2 | Choose two: lakeside/Oishi Park, Chureito viewpoint, or ropeway | Keep local; no mountain outing in poor weather | One wide frame and one detail |
| 7 / Kyoto 1 | Transfer via a verified connection; easy dinner | Treat as a travel/rest day | Evening arrival atmosphere |
| 8 / Kyoto 2 | Higashiyama early, then seated lunch/café and one indoor stop | Reduce temple count | Street layers / architectural detail |
| 9 / Kyoto 3 | Arashiyama early, then lunch and return | Nearby café / indoor browsing | River, greenery, bamboo detail |
| 10 / Kyoto 4 | Uji matcha-focused outing | Central Kyoto tea/café alternative | Tea preparation with permission |
| 11 / Kyoto 5 | Central Kyoto markets, shops, and gift browsing | Keep the day mostly indoors | One purchase and its story |
| 12 / Kyoto 6 | Optional early Daimonji walk OR Kurama–Kibune if conditions and effort suit | Festival only if dates fit, or relaxed city day | Hill panorama or forest sequence |
| 13 / Tokyo 5 | Return to Tokyo; hotel and easy evening | Rest and laundry | Travel transition |
| 14 / Tokyo 6 | Tamiya in Shinbashi; choose Donki or clothing/gift stop, then pack | Limit to the main purchase | Souvenir detail; ask before shop filming |
| 15 / — | Airport transfer and departure | Leave a reliable transfer buffer | Optional final scene |

Night labels count actual nights in each base: Tokyo's final block is its fifth/sixth nights. Arrival/departure times can change what is realistic on Days 1 and 15. The guide's [existing Fuji viewpoints](https://www.mtfujiropeway.jp/en/) include weather-dependent operations; keep a second opportunity for a view during the two-night stay. Summer visibility is not guaranteed.

## Hiking choices

- **Scenic Fuji lakes:** default, with Chureito's stairs an optional effort. [[Japan 2027 — Hiking and Mount Fuji|Existing hiking research]] supplies viewpoint and trail links; its March/April date suggestions do not apply here.
- **Takao Trail 1:** a possible Tokyo hike, shortened with cable-car assistance if operating. Use the [operator's map](https://www.takaotozan.co.jp/course/img/course_map_english.pdf) and current access notices. A summer city visit does not require a full summit walk.
- **Daimonji or Kurama–Kibune:** choose one Kyoto outdoor block; rain, heat, and trail notices can replace it with city activities. Your preferred effort is still unknown.
- **Fuji summit:** open the separate [[Japan 2027 — Summer Fuji Climb Option|climb option]]. The default two-night lake stay does not provide the climb, descent, and recovery block by itself.
- **Kamikochi nature variation:** if alpine-valley scenery matters more than six Kyoto nights, compare **Tokyo 4 → Fuji lakes 2 → Matsumoto 2 → Kyoto 4 → Tokyo 2 = 14 nights**. Use Matsumoto as the access base and spend a full day on a low valley walking route, with current transfers checked. This replaces Kyoto days rather than adding nights. [Official walking routes](https://www.kamikochi.org/thingstodo/walking) · [Access](https://www.kamikochi.org/access). Valley walks do not imply readiness for the high alpine summits. This variant is not scheduled in the default daily table.

## Cafés, shopping, and her gifts

Use [[Japan 2027 — Kichijoji Shopping and Exploring|Kichijoji]] for vintage/records/coffee and [[Japan 2027 — Shopping Shortlist|the shopping research]] for Tamiya and Donki. Kyoto/Uji provide the matcha focus. Choose one clothing piece or compact hobby purchase before optional records/stationery; check actual garment measurements and luggage space.

[[Japan 2027 — Clothing to Browse Online|Your clothing links]] and [[Japan 2027 — Gifts for Her|her clothing, beauty, perfume, and sweets]] remain browsing leads checked in October 2026. Earlier flannels/faux-fur hoodies may be autumn/winter products; summer trip stock, variants, and sizes are unconfirmed. Choose suitable garments for use at home rather than assuming every shop carries them year-round. Record consumption during the trip under meals and take-home food gifts under shopping.

## Phone-ready day rhythm and checklist

**Morning:** main outdoor block when access allows → **midday:** lunch, seated break, café or indoor activity → **later:** short photo walk or one shop → **evening:** dinner, photos backed up, next-day weather/transport checked. This is a flexible rhythm, not an assertion that every attraction opens early.

- [ ] Confirm year, arrival/departure dates, traveler count, hiking effort, and scenic Fuji versus summit.
- [ ] Check hotel air-conditioning, full-stay price, station access, and cancellation deadline.
- [ ] Save dated transport and return connections; verify festival arrangements only after choosing dates.
- [ ] Carry water, sun/rain protection, light city clothing, and grip footwear; use proper mountain layers if hiking.
- [ ] Replace outdoor plans when local heat/weather/access notices make them unsuitable.
- [ ] Compare all actual prices with [[Japan 2027 — Seasonal Planning and Bookings|the seasonal budget]] before purchase.

International flights, dated transport fares, event access, and accommodation remain unquoted. This guide is ready for choosing a route; it is not a confirmed travel schedule.

# 🧳 Japan 2027 — Travel Essentials

**Before choosing dates:** [[Japan 2027 — Preparation Before Choosing Dates|Season-neutral preparation and new source checks · 8 October 2026]]. Season/month remain undecided; older dated examples do not set your departure.

**Maps:** [[Japan 2027 — Maps|📍 All destination, shop, café, and transport searches]]

[[Japan 2027 — Start Here|← Dashboard]] · [[Japan Travel Guide 2027|Full guide]] · [[Japan 2027 — Bookings|Bookings]]

**New official-source checks: 6 October 2026.** These are current rules and preparation steps, not confirmed 2027 requirements. Recheck before paying for flights and before departure. Fill in personal fields after selecting the route.

## 🛂 Entry and arrival preparation

- [ ] Check the requirements for each traveler’s actual passport; departing from Chile does not establish nationality.
- [ ] Check transit-country requirements for the selected connections, including any airport changes or separate tickets.
- [ ] After booking, prepare Visit Japan Web using the actual arrival flight and accommodation details.
- [ ] Save essential travel documents securely offline and confirm how to display the arrival code before departure.

**Passport-specific starting point:** Japan’s current short-stay exemption list includes Chile, with a usual landing-permission period of 90 days for listed countries without a shorter stated period. Use this only if the traveler holds the relevant passport and meets the applicable conditions; nationality is still unconfirmed in your plan. [MOFA short-stay exemption list](https://www.mofa.go.jp/j_info/visit/visa/short/novisa.html) · [Embassy of Japan in Chile](https://www.cl.emb-japan.go.jp/itpr_ja/procedimientodevisado.html)

**Visit Japan Web:** the Digital Agency provides online arrival procedures for immigration and customs. Access it through the official agency page. Completing the form does not replace checking visa eligibility. [Official Visit Japan Web information and access](https://www.digital.go.jp/en/policies/visit_japan_web)

## 🛂 Visa check — passport confirmation pending

**Checked 6 October 2026. Traveler passport nationality and type remain unconfirmed.**

**If traveling on an ordinary Chilean passport for tourism:** Chile appears on Japan’s current visa-exemption list, with a usual short-stay period of up to **90 days**. The proposed 14-night holiday fits that duration. Exemption covers eligible short visits without remunerated professional activity in Japan; it is not permission to work. [Japan MOFA exemption list](https://www.mofa.go.jp/j_info/visit/visa/short/novisa.html) · [Embassy in Chile visa guidance](https://www.cl.emb-japan.go.jp/itpr_ja/procedimientodevisado.html)

**Documents recommended by the Embassy of Japan in Chile:** a valid passport with at least six months remaining, return-flight evidence, and printed accommodation confirmations covering the stay. It also recommends insurance and evidence of financial means. Its approximate US$100/day figure is document-preparation guidance, not a fee or a replacement for your budget. Confirm directly with the embassy if passport validity is close to that recommendation. [Embassy’s visa-exempt traveler checklist](https://www.cl.emb-japan.go.jp/itpr_ja/exencion.html)

- [ ] Confirm the passport country and type for every traveler; do not use Chilean residence as proof of eligibility.
- [ ] Confirm tourism purpose and the actual dates; recheck rules before booking and departure in 2027.
- [ ] Check passport expiry against embassy guidance and the chosen transit route.
- [ ] Save return/onward travel evidence and accommodation confirmations.
- [ ] Check **transit requirements separately** after choosing connection countries and airports.

Visit Japan Web handles arrival procedures; it is not a visa application or a substitute for visa eligibility. Your personal visa status remains pending passport confirmation.

## 📱 Visit Japan Web — prepare before flying

**Checked 6 October 2026; recheck terminal arrangements for 2027.** Online preparation reduces paperwork; it does not guarantee a short queue or replace immigration inspection.

1. Open [the official Visit Japan Web landing page](https://services.digital.go.jp/en/visit-japan-web/) and create an account.
2. Enter your traveler/passport information and the actual arrival flight and first accommodation details.
3. Complete the immigration and customs declarations and have the resulting arrival QR/2D code ready with your passport.
4. Keep the phone charged and save an offline copy of the code as a backup. Update the registration if flight or accommodation details change.

Visit Japan Web supports online arrival procedures. [Digital Agency explanation](https://www.digital.go.jp/en/policies/visit_japan_web)

### What happens after landing

Where integrated kiosks operate, scan your code and passport, complete the photo/fingerprint steps as instructed, and follow the displayed routing to the immigration booth. Collect luggage, then complete customs; eligible passengers may use a facial-recognition customs gate, while others are directed to an officer.

**Current integrated-kiosk locations relevant to your trip:** Haneda terminals **2 and 3**; Narita terminal **3**. The published list does not establish integrated-kiosk availability at Narita terminals 1 or 2. Check your actual arrival terminal and follow staff instructions. [Official terminal list](https://www.customs.go.jp/english/passenger/kiosk_2.pdf)

Integrated kiosks reduce duplicate data submission; dedicated immigration booths still involve inspection. Customs may require additional checks. **Do not plan an airport transfer around a guaranteed processing time.** [Official integrated-kiosk process](https://www.customs.go.jp/english/passenger/Pilot_kiosk.htm)
