## 🧳 Tokyo–Kyoto luggage decision

Measure **length + width + height**, including protruding handles and wheels, before buying the train ticket.

| Suitcase total dimensions | Current JR Central guidance for ordinary luggage |
| --- | --- |
| Up to 160 cm | Oversized-baggage reservation is not required by size; storage must suit the bag and available space |
| Over 160 cm and up to 250 cm | Reserve a seat with an oversized baggage area |
| Over 250 cm | Cannot be brought aboard under these rules; arrange another solution |

JR Central’s summary distinguishes these size bands. Selecting an ordinary reserved seat does not automatically reserve the oversized area. Recheck the current operator rules and any special-item exceptions before booking. [Official JR Central luggage guidance](https://global.jr-central.co.jp/en/info/oversized-baggage/) · [SmartEX baggage booking steps](https://smart-ex.jp/en/entraining/oversized-baggage/)

- **My suitcase measurements / total:** TBD.
- **Carry or forward:** TBD.
- **Train seat and baggage arrangement:** record in [[Japan 2027 — Bookings]].
- **If forwarding:** confirm both hotels accept it, delivery date, full price, and pickup cutoff. Keep essentials with you until confirmed delivery; do not assume same-day service between Tokyo and Kyoto.

## 📲 Apps to prepare on your iPhone

**Official sources checked 6 October 2026.** Recommended for your Tokyo–Kyoto trip using an iPhone and a Chilean account. This is a practical shortlist, not a ranking of app usage among Japanese residents. Recheck App Store region availability, phone requirements, fees, and 2027 features before departure. Use the developer’s official page for download links.

### Google Maps

**Priority:** Essential

**How you will use it:** Open the guide’s saved shops, cafés, sightseeing stops, and transport locations; plan the next journey online

**Setup / official link:** Download your Tokyo/Kyoto areas if available, and save important route screenshots. Offline Maps does not provide walking or public-transport directions. [Google’s iPhone guidance](https://support.google.com/maps/answer/6291838?co=GENIE.Platform%3DiOS&hl=en) · [[Japan 2027 — Maps|Your Maps index]]

### Google Translate (or Apple Translate)

**Priority:** Essential

**How you will use it:** Read signs and menus, type a question, or help with a short conversation

**Setup / official link:** Download Japanese and Spanish in Google Translate before leaving, then test in airplane mode. Available offline functions vary; do not assume every feature works offline. [Google iPhone language downloads](https://support.google.com/translate/answer/6142473?co=GENIE.Platform%3DiOS&hl=en) · [Apple alternative](https://support.apple.com/en-ae/guide/iphone/-iphd74cb450f/ios)

### Apple Wallet + Suica

**Priority:** Essential setup

**How you will use it:** Tap for supported local transport and purchases

**Setup / official link:** Wallet is built in; create Suica there rather than installing the separate Japanese Suica app for basic use. Test a small top-up using your own card; Apple Pay enrollment alone does not confirm that every Chilean debit card can fund Suica. Keep backup cash/card. [Apple setup instructions](https://support.apple.com/ja-jp/108772)

### Safety tips

**Priority:** Essential preparation

**How you will use it:** Receive earthquake, tsunami, and special weather warnings, and access disaster guidance

**Setup / official link:** Set the language, locations, and notification permissions before travel. This is the visitor-focused tool supervised by Japan’s Tourism Agency. [Official agency guidance](https://www.mlit.go.jp/kankocho/seisaku_seido/kihonkeikaku/jizoku_kankochi/anzenkakuho/inbound/tool.html)

### Japan Travel by NAVITIME

**Priority:** Useful addition

**How you will use it:** A second tool for rail/transit route planning, plus nearby stations and ATMs

**Setup / official link:** Choose the visitor version named Japan Travel by NAVITIME. Check which current features are free or require payment before subscribing. [Official app page](https://static.japan.travel.navitime.com/app-lp/en/index.html)

### GO

**Priority:** Useful taxi backup

**How you will use it:** Request a taxi when tired, carrying luggage, or unable to use the planned train

**Setup / official link:** Register and test phone verification before leaving. Official visitor guidance supports non-Japanese phone numbers and international cards/Apple Pay. Confirm your actual card and the fare plus fees. Short-term foreign visitors currently incur an International Service Charge; check the booking screen. [Official visitor app page](https://go.goinc.jp/en) · [Fee explanation](https://support.go.goinc.jp/hc/en-us/articles/46695032643737-What-is-the-International-Service-Charge)

### Tabelog multilingual app

**Priority:** Useful for food

**How you will use it:** Find nearby restaurants/cafés, photos, and available reservations

**Setup / official link:** Use the multilingual version. Browsing can start without signup; online reservations require an account and acceptance of the restaurant’s conditions. If unavailable in the Chilean App Store, use its English website. [Official multilingual app](https://tabelog.com/en/tabelog-multilingual-app/) · [FAQ](https://tabelog.com/en/help)

### LINE

**Priority:** Optional social tool

**How you will use it:** Communicate when a local contact or business specifically uses LINE

**Setup / official link:** Install only if you will use it. For residents of Chile, current account creation uses Continue with Apple or Google; select the country you actually live in. It is not required to ride trains or shop. [Official signup instructions](https://help.line.me/?contentId=20001192)

### Your chosen eSIM app, bank apps, airline app, and Obsidian

**Priority:** Keep ready

**How you will use it:** Manage data, approve payments, access the actual flight booking, and read your guide

**Setup / official link:** Pick the eSIM after comparing its plan. Test bank verification abroad and ensure your vault/confirmations are available offline before leaving; this note does not install apps or configure your phone.


### 🌐 Bookmark these instead of relying on another app

- **Visit Japan Web:** open the official service through Safari and optionally add its shortcut to the Home Screen. Complete the actual arrival details and save the code backup. [Official access](https://services.digital.go.jp/en/visit-japan-web/)
- **SmartEX:** use the official English website for Tokyo–Kyoto bookings. Chile is absent from the currently published list of countries/regions where its international app can be downloaded; do not rely on finding it in your Chilean App Store. [Official app-region FAQ](https://smart-ex.jp/en/faq/category/detail/?id=582) · [English booking service](https://smart-ex.jp/en/)

### 💴 Local payment app you can skip for this holiday

**PayPay:** its official signup requirements exclude overseas phone numbers and require a Japanese mobile number capable of SMS verification. A data-only travel eSIM does not supply that. Use your planned Suica, supported debit-card payments, and cash rather than building your payment plan around a PayPay account. [Official requirements](https://paypay.ne.jp/help/c0032/)

## 📱 Phone and payment backups

These are practical preparation suggestions; test them with your actual devices, bank, and mobile provider.

- [ ] Confirm the phone is unlocked and supports the selected eSIM/SIM; check activation timing, data allowance, and hotspot terms before paying.
- [ ] Decide how to receive bank verification codes abroad; a data-only travel eSIM may not receive calls or SMS sent to your home number. Check roaming charges with your home carrier.
- [ ] Carry a backup physical payment card separately from the main wallet, plus some yen. Confirm overseas use and know the card-freezing procedure.
- [ ] Save hotel addresses in Japanese, confirmations, key contacts, and a simple route offline. Do not rely entirely on live maps or a phone wallet.
- [ ] Prepare secure account-recovery access if the phone is lost; keep recovery codes private and separate from the device.

**If the phone or wallet is lost:** contact the venue/transport operator and local police as appropriate; freeze affected cards and use the device’s lost-mode controls from a trusted device. Use the hotel and [[#📞 Contacts to save offline|contacts card]] for assistance.

## 🎁 Bringing matcha and food souvenirs back to Chile

**Official guidance checked 6 October 2026; recheck before returning in 2027.** SAG requires declaration of plant- and animal-origin products carried into Chile. Declare tea/matcha and relevant food souvenirs; commercial or sealed packaging does not remove the declaration obligation. An inspector determines whether entry is permitted. [SAG traveler declaration guidance](https://www.sag.gob.cl/tramites/declaracion-jurada-sag-sobre-ingreso-de-productos-de-origen-vegetal-o-animal-chile) · [SAG entry rules](https://www.sag.gob.cl/ambitos-de-accion/ingreso-chile)

- [ ] Check the specific products with SAG before buying anything whose admission is uncertain.
- [ ] Keep tea and food in original labeled packaging and accessible for inspection.
- [ ] Prepare the required declaration through [SAG’s official portal](https://dj.sag.gob.cl/) or the accepted arrival procedure; do not guess the opening window for 2027.

- [ ] Test mobile data setup, maps, payment cards, and charging adapters.
- [ ] Confirm suitcase dimensions and any train baggage reservation.
- [ ] Save hotel addresses, insurance contact, and useful numbers offline.
- [ ] Choose the first easy meal and keep arrival-day sightseeing optional.

Use [[Japan 2027 — Today]] for live weather, opening hours, and the route each day. Keep private passport scans and complete booking references out of any publicly shared copy.

## 🗾 Trip at a glance

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

- Departure city: Santiago, Chile (SCL).
- Destination: Tokyo and Kyoto, Japan.
- Length: approximately two weeks in Japan, plus international travel time.
- Priority: cherry blossom photography, with attention to flight and hotel costs.
- Suggested stay: 25 March–8 April 2027, **14 nights**.
- Status: planning draft; no flights, hotels, or trains booked.

**Dates need one final decision:** spending 25 March–8 April in Japan requires departing Santiago earlier. The flight searches already attempted used **departure from Santiago on 25 March and departure from Tokyo on 8 April**, which would give fewer nights in Japan. Compare both options before booking.

## 🌸 When to go

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

Tokyo and Kyoto usually see cherry blossoms around late March and early April. Weather changes the timing each year; the dates above are a planning window, not a confirmed 2027 flowering forecast. [JNTO March guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/march/)

| Period | Why consider it | Main tradeoff |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Late March–early April | Usual Tokyo and Kyoto blossom window | Popular season; compare accommodation carefully |
| Second half of May | Calmer travel after Golden Week, before the rainy season | Main blossoms in Tokyo and Kyoto generally finished |
| June | Off-season option | Rainy season |
| January–February weekdays, outside New Year | Off-season option | Cold weather and no spring sakura |

Sources: [JNTO seasonal tips](https://www.japan.travel/en/gc/tips/), [JNTO May guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/may/).

Avoid late April and the first week of May if reducing holiday crowds is important. Golden Week brings packed trains and hotels. It is separate from the usual Tokyo–Kyoto blossom window. [Golden Week guidance](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/may/)

## 🗓️ Suggested 14-night route

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

This is a proposed itinerary. Shift photography days according to actual flowering and weather.

| Dates in Japan | Base | Nights | Focus |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 25–31 March | Tokyo | 6 | Arrival recovery, city exploration, sakura photography |
| 31 March–6 April | Kyoto | 6 | Temples, canals, blossoms, optional nearby excursion |
| 6–8 April | Tokyo | 2 | Flexible photo day, shopping, departure preparation |

### Tokyo: first six nights

- Day 1: arrive, check in, take a short neighborhood walk, and rest.
- Day 2: easy city exploration and a first blossom check.
- Day 3: Chidorigafuchi photography; retain an evening option if seasonal illumination is operating.
- Day 4: Shinjuku Gyoen or Ueno Park, depending on current flowering.
- Day 5: Meguro River and a relaxed neighborhood walk.
- Day 6: flexible photography day for weather, peak blooms, or a location worth revisiting.
- Transfer day: travel to Kyoto and settle in.

Chidorigafuchi has a cherry-lined moat walkway and seasonal night illumination. Shinjuku Gyoen, Ueno Park, and Meguro River are also highlighted by JNTO. Check 2027 opening hours, entry requirements, and illumination schedules closer to departure. [Chidorigafuchi](https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/1736/), [Spring guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/guide/spring-guide/)

### Kyoto: six nights

- First evening: a relaxed walk near the accommodation.
- Full day 1: Philosopher’s Path and the surrounding eastern Kyoto area.
- Full day 2: a temple-focused day with an early start.
- Full day 3: flexible blossom photography; use Kyoto’s current flowering information.
- Full day 4: optional Nara or Osaka excursion, or another Kyoto day.
- Full day 5: revisit the strongest photo location, then prepare for the return journey.

The Philosopher’s Path follows a canal beneath cherry trees. Kyoto’s tourism office publishes a blossom calendar; the page currently covers 2026, so check for its 2027 update. [Philosopher’s Path](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/philosophers-path-tetsugakunomichi/), [Kyoto blossom calendar](https://kyoto.travel/en/seasonal-info/cherryblossom/)

### Tokyo: final two nights

- Return with room for a final photo session or shopping.
- Keep the last day light and check the airport transfer against the flight departure time.
- Alternative: compare arrival in Tokyo with departure from Osaka Kansai (KIX), which could remove the return journey to Tokyo. Compare the total flight and ground-transport cost before choosing.

## 🔎 Official schedule check for 2027

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

**Checked 6 October 2026 for the proposed 25 March–8 April 2027 trip.** The sources below provide useful current operating rules, but most spring event pages still show earlier seasons. No exact-date train service, flight, or seasonal event has been booked or independently confirmed here. “Not verified” means this check did not establish a published 2027 schedule; it does not prove that no announcement exists anywhere.

### Shinjuku Gyoen · 28 March

**Finding from the official source:** Current general hours for this season are 09:00–18:00, last entry 17:30; the garden lists daily opening during 25 March–24 April. Busy cherry-season dates may need reservations. No 2027-specific reservation notice was verified. [Garden admission](https://policies.env.go.jp/national-garden/shinjukugyoen/english/guide/information/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** A 09:00 morning visit is a reasonable provisional target. Recheck the Sunday reservation requirement before fixing this day. Current hours are not a guarantee against 2027 changes or exceptional closure.

### Chidorigafuchi illumination · 27 March or a flexible evening

**Finding from the official source:** The official page still shows the 2026 season, rather than a verified 2027 announcement. [Chiyoda illumination page](https://visit-chiyoda.tokyo/sakura/spot/light.php)

**What this means for the itinerary:** Keep the daytime photo session. Treat the evening as optional until 2027 dates and crowd routing are announced.

### Kyoto blossom reports · 31 March–6 April

**Finding from the official source:** The official calendar is titled 2026 and says that season’s updates ended on 10 April 2026. [Kyoto blossom calendar](https://kyoto.travel/en/seasonal-info/cherryblossom/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** It provides previous-season context, not a 2027 flowering forecast. Choose photo locations from new local reports nearer travel.

### Kyoto spring illuminations · Kyoto stay

**Finding from the official source:** The tourism office’s spring illumination announcement is dated 31 March 2026. A 2027 list was not verified. [Kyoto spring events](https://kyoto.travel/en/news/spring-illumination-news/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** Leave Kyoto evenings flexible; do not attach a paid night visit to a specific date yet.

### Kiyomizu-dera night viewing · optional 2 April

**Finding from the official source:** The temple’s visit page explicitly labels its event calendar 2026. A 2027 spring night-viewing schedule was not verified. [Temple events](https://www.kiyomizudera.or.jp/en/visit/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** Retain the daytime visit. Add night viewing only after the temple publishes dates covering the actual Kyoto stay.

### Tokyo–Kyoto train · 31 March; return · 6 April

**Finding from the official source:** SmartEX permits certain reservations up to one year ahead, with limited eligibility and inventory. Beyond one month, train times and assigned seats are provisional. [Booking periods](https://smart-ex.jp/en/reservation/useful/accept_time/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** Early booking eligibility is not confirmation of the final timetable. Review the outbound booking in late February and the return booking on 6 March 2027.

### Seasonal Shinkansen fare calendar

**Finding from the official source:** The checked SmartEX page presents 2025 and 2026 calendars; a 2027 calendar was not verified. [Seasonal calendar](https://smart-ex.jp/en/product/set_dates/)

**What this means for the itinerary:** Keep intercity prices as TBD until a quote for the actual travel date is obtained.


### Rail booking details and source differences 🚄

The checked English SmartEX page excludes oversized-baggage seats from one-year advance reservations. Its Japanese booking-period page lists fewer exclusions, so confirm the exact product and baggage-seat eligibility in the booking flow before relying on an early reservation. The English and Japanese pages also give different notification times on the one-month date; rely on the reservation confirmation rather than promising a specific notification hour. [English booking rules](https://smart-ex.jp/en/reservation/useful/accept_time/), [Japanese booking rules](https://www.smart-ex.jp/reservation/useful/accept_time/).

**Suggested recheck dates, not announced publication dates:**

- [ ] **December 2026–January 2027:** look for the 2027 railway timetable announcement and updated seasonal fare calendar.
- [ ] **February 2027:** revisit official spring-event and garden reservation pages.
- [ ] **Late February 2027:** check the 31 March outbound train’s one-month window in SmartEX. February has no 31st, so verify the operator’s handling of this date in the app or official guidance.
- [ ] **6 March 2027:** review the 6 April return train’s confirmed time, seat, baggage arrangements, and fare.
- [ ] **March 2027:** recheck Chidorigafuchi, Kiyomizu-dera, Kyoto illumination announcements, and the current blossom calendar.
- [ ] **One week before departure and each evening during travel:** verify actual opening hours, weather, closures, and flowering reports.

Airport train and bus departures require the confirmed flight time, terminal, and hotel address before a specific service can be selected. Meguro River illumination, optional museums, and other attraction-specific 2027 access remain unverified in this check; consult each venue before adding a timed reservation.

## 🧭 Detailed daily itinerary

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

This working schedule assumes **arrival in Japan on 25 March 2027 and departure on 8 April 2027**: 14 nights across 15 calendar dates. All times below are suggested activity blocks, not confirmed opening hours or transport schedules. Move outdoor days within each city to follow blossoms and weather. Final routes depend on the hotel address, mobility preferences, and booked flights.

### 25 March · Arrival in Tokyo 🛬

**Main goal:** arrive comfortably and recover. Complete the airport checklist, travel to the hotel, and leave luggage only if storage is confirmed. After check-in, take a 30–60 minute neighborhood walk if energy allows. Choose dinner close to the hotel and prepare a small camera bag for tomorrow. If the flight arrives late, skip sightseeing entirely. **Night 1: Tokyo.**

### 26 March · Asakusa and an easy city introduction 🏯

**Morning:** an unhurried Senso-ji and Asakusa visit; allow roughly 2–3 hours including breaks. **Lunch:** choose a nearby restaurant before adding another district. **Afternoon:** a short local walk or return to the hotel; use this as the jet-lag recovery day. **Evening:** easy dinner and a blossom check for tomorrow. If blooms are already at their best elsewhere, swap this day with a photo day. **Night 2: Tokyo.**

### 27 March · Chidorigafuchi photography 🌸

**Morning:** allow a 2–3 hour photo block for moat views, branches, and reflections without blocking the walkway. **Midday:** lunch and a rest or image-backup session. **Afternoon:** optional short exploration in the same area rather than another distant attraction. **Evening:** return only if illumination and access are confirmed. In sustained rain, substitute an indoor visit and reschedule the main photo session. **Night 3: Tokyo.**

### 28 March · Shinjuku Gyoen and Shinjuku 📸

**Morning:** visit the garden at a verified entry time, with roughly 2–3 hours for compositions and a relaxed walk. **Lunch:** in the surrounding Shinjuku area. **Afternoon:** neighborhood exploration or recovery time. **Evening:** optional city scenes, subject to energy. This is a Sunday, so check cherry-season reservation arrangements before assigning it permanently. The official garden guidance says busy blossom-season dates may require advance reservations; **2027 requirements still need checking**. [Official Shinjuku Gyoen admission guidance](https://policies.env.go.jp/national-garden/shinjukugyoen/english/guide/information/) **Night 4: Tokyo.**

### 29 March · Meguro River and Nakameguro 🌆

**Morning:** relaxed breakfast and a free block for laundry, shopping, or an earlier missed photo session. **Afternoon:** explore Nakameguro and photograph the river, allowing time to wait for light. **Evening:** dinner nearby; add night photography only if seasonal lighting is confirmed. Follow crowd directions and avoid stopping on busy bridges. If the blossoms are better at Ueno, substitute Ueno rather than visiting both. **Night 5: Tokyo.**

### 30 March · Tokyo blossom buffer and packing 🔄

Choose **one** priority: revisit the best blossom location, visit Ueno, or take the Yanaka–Nezu alternative if you want neighborhood scenes. Keep the afternoon light enough to pack, charge devices, and confirm the Kyoto ticket. Back up photographs separately from the memory card. Set aside snacks and essentials for the train. **Night 6: Tokyo.**

### 31 March · Tokyo to Kyoto 🚄

Check out, travel to the booked Shinkansen departure station, and allow a suggested **30–45 minute station buffer** for unfamiliar exits, locating platforms, and buying food. Use the actual train timetable to calculate hotel arrival; do not treat the station buffer as the full transfer time. After reaching Kyoto, store luggage if arranged and keep sightseeing modest. A short local walk and dinner are sufficient. **Night 7: Kyoto.**

### 1 April · Philosopher’s Path and eastern Kyoto 🌸

**Morning:** devote a relaxed 2–3 hour block to the canal and photo stops. **Lunch:** near the walking area. **Afternoon:** add at most one nearby temple after verifying access, or return for a rest. **Evening:** easy dinner rather than a compulsory cross-city outing. If blossoms are not ready, swap with the Fushimi Inari day and keep this slot available. **Night 8: Kyoto.**

### 2 April · Kiyomizu-dera, historic streets, and Yasaka 🏯

**Morning:** begin at Kiyomizu-dera during verified visiting hours. Allow time for slopes and steps, then continue through the surrounding historic streets. **Midday:** lunch and a seated break. **Afternoon:** Yasaka Shrine and a respectful Gion walk if energy allows. These are linked sightseeing areas, but walking still takes time; reduce the route if crowds or fatigue build. Follow all private-lane and photography signs. **Night 9: Kyoto.**

### 3 April · Fushimi Inari and a flexible afternoon ⛩️

**Morning:** allow 2–3 hours for a visit at your own pace; a full mountain walk is optional. Turn back when comfortable rather than treating the summit as compulsory. **Afternoon:** rest, an indoor cultural visit, or a blossom session chosen from current reports. **Evening:** a local meal and photo backup. Keep this day adjustable because it falls on a Saturday. **Night 10: Kyoto.**

### 4 April · Choose Uji, Nara, Osaka, or more Kyoto 🦌

Make the decision from blossom conditions and energy, preferably the previous evening.

- **Uji:** choose Byodoin, one tea stop, and a river walk; see [[#🍵 Uji — the strongest new option for your interests|the Uji plan]]. This replaces the other excursion options.
- **Nara:** make Nara Park and Todai-ji the main anchors; allow an unhurried day and check the route from the chosen arrival station. Give deer space and follow local feeding instructions.
- **Osaka:** choose either a daytime Osaka Castle Park visit plus Dotonbori, or a lighter afternoon/evening city outing. Check the last practical return to Kyoto before dinner.
- **Kyoto:** keep the day for peak blooms or a recovery day if the trip feels busy.

Do not combine Nara and Osaka in this photo-focused schedule. **Night 11: Kyoto.**

### 5 April · Final Kyoto photo day or Arashiyama 🎋

**First choice:** revisit the strongest blossom area if conditions are good. **Alternative:** dedicate the morning to Arashiyama, keeping nearby stops together instead of crossing Kyoto repeatedly. **Afternoon:** lunch, a relaxed final walk, and packing. Ohara can replace this outing if countryside scenery matters more, but keep it as a separate day plan with verified transport. **Night 12: Kyoto.**

### 6 April · Return to Tokyo 🧳

Check out and take the confirmed Shinkansen service. After checking in or storing luggage, choose a short nearby outing and dinner. Avoid an essential timed attraction immediately after the train, since delays and hotel logistics could consume the buffer. Confirm the airport, terminal, departure time, and luggage arrangements for 8 April. **Night 13: Tokyo.**

### 7 April · Last flexible Tokyo day 🎁

Use the morning for a final photo location or shopping, choosing one main area. Keep the afternoon available for packing, photo backup, and checking the airport route. Have an early, convenient dinner. If the flight leaves very early on 8 April, consider whether an airport-area hotel would simplify departure, then update the booking plan. **Night 14: Tokyo.**

### 8 April · Departure ✈️

Work backward from the airline’s required airport arrival time. Add the confirmed transfer duration, hotel-to-station walk, and a disruption buffer. Check the terminal before setting out. Keep passport, boarding information, and required documents accessible. Sightseeing is optional only if the flight schedule comfortably permits it. **No hotel night counted for this date.**

## 🚉 Transport and luggage details

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

### Airport transfer decision

**Airport Maps:** [Haneda (HND)](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Haneda+Airport+Tokyo+Japan) · [Narita (NRT)](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Narita+International+Airport+Japan) · [Kansai (KIX), optional departure](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Kansai+International+Airport+Osaka+Japan) · [Santiago (SCL)](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Arturo+Merino+Benitez+International+Airport+Santiago+Chile)

Select the terminal shown on your ticket; airport links do not identify an airline’s check-in counter.

| Arrival airport | Options to compare | How to choose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Haneda HND | Keikyu, Tokyo Monorail, airport bus, or taxi | Compare the exact hotel route, changes, service hours, and luggage handling |
| Narita NRT | Narita Express, Keisei services, airport bus, or taxi | Compare the destination station and onward journey; the airport is farther from central Tokyo |

Start with the [official Tokyo airport-access guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/plan/airport-access/index.html), then check the operator’s actual date and time. The lowest fare may involve more changes; a direct service may suit a tired arrival better.

### Intercity booking checklist

- [ ] Record the exact departure station, train, carriage, seat, departure time, and arrival time.
- [ ] Confirm the ticket’s entry method rather than assuming your local IC card is enough.
- [ ] Measure suitcase length + width + height and check the operator’s baggage rules before selecting seats.
- [ ] Reserve the required baggage accommodation if your suitcase falls in the oversized category.
- [ ] Arrange hotel storage or delivery separately; confirm delivery lead time before sending bags.
- [ ] Keep travel documents, camera, valuables, and overnight essentials with you.

SmartEX has specific oversized-baggage seat requirements; consult its current dimensions and exceptions when booking rather than relying on airline baggage labels. [Official Shinkansen baggage rules](https://smart-ex.jp/en/entraining/oversized-baggage/)

In Kyoto, compare rail or subway plus a short walk with bus-only journeys. Travel without large luggage when sightseeing; the city recommends luggage services and combining transport modes. [Getting around Kyoto](https://kyoto.travel/en/getting-around/), [Kyoto luggage services](https://kyoto.travel/en/luggage-services/).

## 🍜 Meals and daily rhythm

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

Plan meals around the day’s neighborhood so hunger does not trigger another cross-city journey. Keep one nearby fallback for dinner, especially after transfers. Specific restaurants can be selected once hotel addresses, dietary needs, and the meal budget are known.

- **Breakfast:** keep it simple before photo mornings; confirm hotel breakfast hours if relying on it.
- **Lunch:** allow a suggested 45–60 minute break near the main sightseeing area, with more time for queues.
- **Dinner:** choose one planned meal or an easy neighborhood option. Reserve only if the particular restaurant requires it or is a trip priority.
- **Snacks and water:** carry enough for photo walks and train transfers, respecting rules for eating at each location.
- **Recovery:** include a seated break and occasional early night. Skip the optional evening before cutting sleep repeatedly.

Track actual meal spending daily against the food budget. List allergies and other dietary restrictions before choosing venues; use the phrase section and a checked Japanese dietary card.

## 📍 Recommended places and Google Maps

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

This shortlist prioritizes your interests: cherry blossom photography first, then classic sights and optional excursions. The priorities and suggested pacing are recommendations, not a requirement to visit every place.

**Map links open Google Maps searches for named locations.** They are not verified entrance pins or walking routes. Check the selected result and entrance before navigating; parks, rivers, and neighborhoods cover larger areas.

### Tokyo

| [Todai-ji](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Todaiji%20Temple%20Nara%20Japan) | Nara day trip | A major historic temple and Great Buddha visit, well suited to a culture-focused excursion. | Allow an unhurried visit |
| [Dotonbori](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Dotonbori%20Osaka%20Japan) | Osaka evening option | Neon signs and an urban food outing provide a strong contrast to Kyoto. | Late afternoon and evening |
| [Osaka Castle Park](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Osaka%20Castle%20Park%20Osaka%20Japan) | Osaka daytime option | A park and castle setting to consider if you choose a fuller Osaka day. | Before an evening in Dotonbori |

### How to fit the shortlist into the trip

- **Tokyo blossom sessions:** pick Chidorigafuchi, Shinjuku Gyoen, and Meguro River according to current flowering. Keep Ueno as another option rather than treating all four as compulsory.
- **Tokyo culture session:** give Asakusa and Senso-ji a half-day when you want a break from blossom hunting.
- **Kyoto eastern area:** plan the Philosopher’s Path separately from a Kiyomizu-dera–Yasaka–Gion walk so there is time for photographs.
- **Kyoto other areas:** reserve separate blocks for Fushimi Inari and Arashiyama; make Nijo Castle optional.
- **Day trips:** choose Nara for historic temples and park scenery, or Osaka for a city and food contrast. Keep the excursion flexible if Kyoto blossoms are at their best.

Check 2027 opening hours, admission prices, reservation requirements, and seasonal illumination before locking in a day. Blossom conditions apply to each location, not just the city as a whole.

Official sources supporting the shortlist: [Tokyo sightseeing guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/see-and-do/index.html), [Tokyo spring guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/story/guide/spring/index.html), [Chidorigafuchi](https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/1736/), [Kyoto destinations](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/), [Kyoto sample itineraries](https://kyoto.travel/en/itineraries), [Philosopher’s Path](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/philosophers-path-tetsugakunomichi/), [JNTO Nara guide](https://www.japan.travel/en/destinations/kansai/nara/), [Official Todai-ji guide](https://www.visitnara.jp/venues/A00485/), [JNTO Kansai itinerary](https://www.japan.travel/en/in/itineraries/kansai-new/).

## 💵 US$5,000 budget and affordable food plan

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

**Working assumption:** one traveler, 14 nights and up to 15 days in Japan. International flights are excluded. US$5,000 is the planning ceiling being explored, not a commitment to spend it or a confirmed final budget. Dates and bookings remain undecided.

Aim to spend around **US$3,850**, keeping **US$1,150 in reserve**. These are spending targets, not live hotel quotes or verified 2027 fares. Yen purchases must be converted at the actual card or cash exchange rate, including fees.

| Category | Suggested allowance USD | Practical target |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Accommodation | 2,100 | Average $150 × 14 nights, including applicable taxes and fees |
| Meals and drinks | 600 | Average $40 × 15 calendar days; simple meals leave room for selected restaurant dinners |
| Intercity trains | 250 | Allowance for Tokyo–Kyoto and return; replace with actual dated fares |
| Local transport and airport transfers | 200 | Prefer public transport; track taxis separately |
| Attractions and selected experiences | 250 | Prioritize activities you care about rather than filling every day |
| Connectivity and insurance | 150 | Replace with personal quotes and coverage terms |
| Shopping and miscellaneous | 300 | Include laundry and small extras; avoid using the reserve for impulse purchases |
| **Planned spending** | **3,850** | An allocation to test against real quotes |
| **Unspent reserve** | **1,150** | Hotel overruns, exchange-rate changes, disruptions, or other unexpected costs |
| **Available budget** | **5,000** | Flights separate |

### Where to spend a little more 🏨

Prioritize a conveniently located hotel, comfortable sleep, and manageable arrival and departure transfers. Compare the total hotel price and actual station walk before paying more for location. Choose two or three special meals or experiences rather than making every dinner expensive. Keep comfortable walking shoes and camera rain protection in the packing plan.

### Where to save 🍙

Convenience stores and casual chains are useful everyday options, even with a larger budget. Mix **7-Eleven and FamilyMart** breakfasts or lunches with affordable sit-down meals. **Yoshinoya and Sukiya** are beef-bowl chain options to compare near the hotel; these are additional meal options. The user later confirmed the sushi chain they meant was Sushiro.

Current official menus and product lists checked on **6 October 2026** support using these as low-cost options, but prices and products vary by branch, region, and season:

- **FamilyMart:** its official page includes a rice-ball-and-side-dish set at **¥450 including tax** for listed Tohoku/Kanto regions, and a set at **¥598 including tax** for listed Kansai/Chugoku/Shikoku regions. These illustrate inexpensive choices; they are not guaranteed stock at a particular Tokyo or Kyoto branch. [FamilyMart rice-ball products](https://www.family.co.jp/goods/omusubi.html)
- **7-Eleven:** compare the local branch’s bento, rice balls, sandwiches, and sides. The official product page separates regional availability, so do not assume a national listing is sold near your hotel. [7-Eleven bento products](https://www.sej.co.jp/products/a/cat/010020010000000/)
- **Yoshinoya:** compare a basic beef bowl with meal sets and extras using the Japan menu. [Official Yoshinoya menu](https://www.yoshinoya.com/menu/gyudon/gyu-don/)
- **Sukiya:** compare the basic bowl and sets at the actual branch. Search snippets and the opened mobile page showed conflicting prices in this check, so no exact current bowl price is recorded here. [Official Sukiya menu](https://www.sukiya.jp/sp/menu/out/gyudon/100100/index.html)

Suggested allowances are per person and should be adjusted for appetite and dietary needs.

Adjust portions and choices to your needs; include some variety and check ingredients for dietary restrictions. Alcohol and specialty cafés can increase the daily total. If hotel breakfast costs extra, compare its price with nearby alternatives.

Use the earlier economical allocation: accommodation **$1,400**, meals **$525**, intercity trains **$220**, local/airport transport **$180**, attractions **$150**, connectivity/insurance **$125**, shopping **$100**, and reserve **$300**. This totals **$3,000**, excluding flights, and assumes an average hotel cost of **$100 a night** is achievable for the final dates.

Reduce hotel cost, shopping, taxis, and optional premium experiences before relying on food savings alone. For scale, saving $10 on food each day saves $150 over 15 days; saving $40 per hotel night saves $560 over 14 nights. Keep the reserve intact where possible. If suitable blossom-season hotel quotes exceed the target, revise accommodation choices or the overall budget before booking.

### 🍵 Choosing your matcha experience

**Doutor nearby searches:** [Tokyo](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Doutor+Coffee+Tokyo+Japan) · [Kyoto](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Doutor+Coffee+Kyoto+Japan) — chain comparison only; no branch selected.

For a lower-cost sweet milky drink, compare the Starbucks matcha latte with local alternatives. For tea without the latte format, try **usucha**, a thinner whisked matcha, at Nakamura Tokichi. **Koicha** is a thicker preparation and costs more on this menu. A float or parfait is a sweet dessert experience and should be budgeted separately from an ordinary cup of tea. The tea-menu image distinguishes the two preparations. [Official matcha menu image](https://cdn.shopify.com/s/files/1/0636/5718/1397/files/261001_ginza_menu_03.jpg?v=1790670605)

For an economical coffee benchmark elsewhere, Doutor’s July 2026 announcement lists a small blend coffee at **¥300** after its price revision. This is a chain benchmark, not a verified Ginza branch quote or selected café location. [Official price announcement](https://www.doutor.co.jp/news/newsrelease/detail/20260701110453.html)

### 🗓️ A relaxed Ginza café outing

Use a flexible Tokyo afternoon, such as **7 April if the proposed dates remain**, for Ginza. Choose **one coffee stop plus one matcha stop**, rather than visiting every café. Sanjikken and Nakamura Tokichi are a practical pair to explore, with shopping or a neighborhood walk between them. Choose Ginza West instead of Sanjikken if a classic seated café and cake appeal more. Keep Bongen optional if the queue or price is unsuitable.

A coffee of the day at Sanjikken plus usucha at Nakamura totals **¥2,370 at the displayed prices**, before food or travel. Coffee plus the matcha parfait totals **¥3,370**. Set a suggested **¥3,000–4,000 limit for this outing**, adjusting to the actual menu and tax treatment; a premium parfait or extra drink can exceed that target.

For everyday cafés, allow a suggested **¥500–1,000 per visit**, then budget separately for specialist tea and dessert stops. Fifteen visits at ¥700 would be **¥10,500**; this is budget arithmetic, not a promise that every selected café costs ¥700. Charge cafés to the existing meals/drinks allowance so the budget does not silently expand.

## 🛍️ Clothing and souvenirs to remember the trip

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

Choose a few things you will use or display at home. The suggestions below fit the current Tokyo–Kyoto route; price allowances are personal spending targets, not verified product quotes.

### 👕 Clothing ideas

- **Everyday clothes:** browse UNIQLO in Ginza for a T-shirt, light layer, or trousers you can keep wearing after the trip. Try items on and compare measurements rather than assuming your usual size transfers directly. The Ginza flagship is a convenient addition to the café outing. [Official Ginza shopping guide](https://www.ginza.jp/en/landmark/9743)
- **Something with character:** allow a relaxed Shimokitazawa session for a vintage jacket, overshirt, or other secondhand piece. Check condition, fit, and care labels; vintage does not automatically mean cheap. [Official Shimokitazawa shopping walk](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/story/walks-and-tours/shimo-kitazawa/)
- **Japanese craft detail:** consider an indigo-dyed garment, a patterned textile accessory, or a small Nishijin-woven item in Kyoto. Ask where and how it was made. Kyoto's official souvenir guide highlights dyed goods, woven accessories, and braided cords. [Kyoto craft ideas](https://shugakuryoko.kyoto.travel/walk_gift/)
- **Traditional clothing:** if you want a yukata or haori, make it a deliberate purchase. Ask staff to explain sizing, material, required accessories, and care before paying. A secondhand garment can be memorable, but inspect stains and wear. No specific clothing shop or price is confirmed yet.

### 📏 Clothing sizes: personal shopping reference

**Your starting information:** usual upper-body size **L**; trouser label approximately **EU 44–46**, confirmed by you. The guidance below uses men's clothing as a provisional reference; women's and unisex charts must be checked separately. No exact Japanese size is confirmed yet.

There is no reliable universal rule that a foreign L always equals a Japanese XL. Both UNIQLO Japan and GU say that garments with the same letter can have different finished measurements depending on the design. Compare the actual item, especially slim versus oversized fits. [UNIQLO size guidance](https://faq.uniqlo.com/articles/Knowledge/100001976/) · [GU size guidance](https://faq.gu-global.com/articles/Knowledge/100009031)

| Item | Practical starting point for you | Measurements to check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 👕 T-shirts / shirts | Compare **L and XL** in the fitting room; this is a trial strategy, not a confirmed conversion. Oversized L may already be roomy. | Chest/body chart, garment width, shoulders and length. |
| 🧥 Jackets / coats | Compare L and XL over the layer you intend to wear underneath. | Shoulders, chest ease, sleeve length; zip or button the jacket and move your arms. |
| 👖 Jeans / trousers | Your confirmed EU 44–46 is a starting point: compare roughly W30–W32 and Japanese letter sizes S/M using the exact waist chart. These are trial candidates, not guaranteed equivalents. | Waist, hips, thigh room, rise and inseam. Numeric labels may mean inches, centimetres or a brand's size code. |
| 🩲 Underwear | Use the specific product's **body-waist** range; do not copy your T-shirt size. | As one product-chart example, UNIQLO Japan AIRism boxer briefs list L for waist **84–92 cm**; recheck the current item's chart before buying. Ask about fitting and return rules before opening a pack. [Product reference](https://www.uniqlo.com/jp/ja/products/E442798-000/00) |
| ♻️ Secondhand clothes | At 2nd STREET and RAGTAG, compare each garment individually. | Mixed brands, countries and older cuts make a single conversion unreliable. Check actual dimensions and fit, including possible shrinkage. |

**Confirmed: your trousers are EU 44–46.** Using men's regular trousers as the reference, ARKET groups EU 44–46 with a body waist of **76–80 cm** and UK trouser labels 30R–32R. BOSS's regular chart gives EU 44 jeans/chino widths **29/30**, and EU 46 widths **31/32**. The charts differ, so use roughly **W30–W32 as an initial fitting-room search**, with W29 possible at the smaller end. This is a provisional comparison for shopping at Japanese stores, not an exact national conversion. [ARKET men's chart](https://www.arket.com/en-eu/customer-service/size-guide/men/) · [BOSS regular trousers chart](https://www.hugoboss.com/uk/size-charts/menswear-trousers/size-chart_BOSS_btm8.html)

At UNIQLO or GU, compare **S and M bottoms** against your actual waist and the individual product chart. For underwear, UNIQLO AIRism boxer briefs list **M for a body waist of 76–84 cm**, making M a useful candidate if your measurement falls in that interval. [Official product chart reference](https://www.uniqlo.com/jp/ja/products/E474299-000/00) Do not default to L simply because your shirts are L. Your upper-body L and smaller trouser label can be perfectly valid; choose each category independently. The provisional conversion above assumes men's sizing; women's EU 44–46 requires a different chart.

### 🧵 Measurements to save before departure

Measure your body chest, waist and hips in centimetres, and note your height. Also lay a well-fitting T-shirt and trousers flat: record shirt width from armpit to armpit, shoulder width and length; trouser waistband width and inseam. Double the flat waistband width to compare garment circumference. Keep body and garment measurements separate: a shirt needs room beyond your body chest. UNIQLO explicitly distinguishes these two charts. [Official measuring instructions](https://faq.uniqlo.com/articles/FAQ/100004162)

| Waist measurement in inches | Equivalent centimetres — arithmetic only |
| --- | --- |
| 30 | 76.2 |
| 32 | 81.3 |
| 34 | 86.4 |
| 35 | 88.9 |
| 36 | 91.4 |
| 38 | 96.5 |
| 40 | 101.6 |
| 44 | 111.8 |
| 46 | 116.8 |

These conversions use 1 inch = 2.54 cm; they do not promise that a garment labelled W34 has an actual waistband of exactly 86.4 cm. Check whether a chart describes body measurements or the finished garment.

**Shopping logistics:** UNIQLO Japan lists men's sizes up to 4XL overall, but some sizes are online-only and availability varies by item. Check branch stock before depending on a larger size during a short visit. [Official available sizes](https://faq.uniqlo.com/articles/Knowledge/100001541/)

**Useful label words:** サイズ = size; ウエスト = waist; チェスト = chest; 身幅 = flat garment width; 肩幅 = shoulder width; 袖丈 = sleeve length; 股下 = inseam; ヌード寸法 = body measurements; 仕上がり寸法 = finished garment measurements. Ask **“Shichaku shite mo ii desu ka?”** — “May I try it on?”

| Idea | Why it makes a good memory | What to check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Furoshiki wrapping cloth | A decorative, reusable cloth for wrapping or carrying items | Size, fabric, care, and maker; Musubi is a specialist to browse |
| Tenugui cotton cloth | Compact and light; choose a design tied to a place you visited | Whether you will use it as a cloth or display it; ask about washing |
| Folding fan | A portable traditional design object | Material, construction, and origin |
| Small ceramic cup or bowl | Something useful for coffee, tea, or meals at home | Maker, care instructions, and protective packaging |
| Nishijin textile accessory or kumihimo braid | A Kyoto craft memory without purchasing a full formal outfit | Material and provenance |
| Washi stationery, notebook, or postcards | Ideal for travel notes and a keepsake folder | Pick a few designs rather than buying duplicates |
| A small artwork or print | A lasting reminder of a favorite scene | Whether it is a reproduction or an original; framing and packing needs |

For furoshiki, start with [Musubi's official shop directory](https://www.musubi-online.com/f/shoplist). For regional crafts and sellers, consult [Kyoto's official souvenir directory](https://ja.kyoto.travel/tourism/search.php?category_id=5). The ideas are recommendations; check actual inventory before making a special journey.

### 📍 Fit shopping into the itinerary

- **Asakusa day:** browse Nakamise for traditional goods and small souvenirs while visiting Senso-ji. Shop during business hours rather than expecting temple-walk access to mean shops are open. [Official Nakamise guide](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/spot/73/index.html)
- **Ginza café afternoon:** combine clothes browsing with **Ginza Itoya** for stationery. Use the actual flagship listing for current floors and hours. [Official Itoya location](https://www.ito-ya.co.jp/ext/store/ginza/ginza/index.html)
- **Optional Shimokitazawa outing:** make vintage clothing the main activity and add one café; avoid squeezing it into a distant sightseeing day.
- **Kyoto:** add a shopping block around Shijo–Kawaramachi or select a craft shop in the day's area. The city guide identifies Shijo as a major shopping district. [Official central Kyoto guide](https://kyoto.travel/en/areas/central/)
- **Final Tokyo day:** buy only the remaining priorities, then pack. Earlier shopping leaves more time to compare and avoid hurried airport purchases.

**Map searches:** confirm the selected shop or area before navigating.

### 🧾 Tax-free shopping for the 2027 trip

**Checked 6 October 2026.** These existing stops currently advertise tax-free service; recheck branch participation and procedures before your 2027 visit.

| Existing stop | Verified current status | Official source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UNIQLO Ginza | UNIQLO advertises tax-free shopping at all Japan stores and specifically features Ginza | [UNIQLO tax-free page](https://www.uniqlo.com/jp/ja/special-feature/tax-free) |
| 2nd STREET Shimokitazawa · 2-30-13 Kitazawa | The exact branch page lists Tax-free and confirms the service | [Branch listing](https://www.2ndstreet.jp/shop/details?shopsId=31139) |
| 2nd STREET Shijo Kawaramachi · 538-1 Nakanocho | The exact branch page lists Tax-free and confirms the service | [Branch listing](https://www.2ndstreet.jp/shop/details?shopsId=30860) |

**Not established in this check:** the guide’s exact Ginza Itoya, RAGTAG Kyoto, Musubi, Ippodo, and Marukyu Koyamaen branches. Ask the chosen shop; unverified does not mean unavailable. Nakamise and Shimokitazawa are areas with multiple independent shops, not one tax-free retailer.

**Your trip falls under the new refund system starting 1 November 2026:** pay the tax-inclusive amount at the participating store, register the eligible purchase, then obtain customs confirmation that the goods are leaving Japan within **90 days of purchase**. The retailer/operator refunds the tax after confirmation. The revised minimum eligible purchase is **¥5,000 before tax**. Ask the shop about aggregation rules, refund method, timing, and any fees. [Official NTA reform leaflet](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/shohi/menzei/202506/pdf/0025006-106.pdf)

- [ ] Bring the actual passport and confirm visitor eligibility with the shop before payment.
- [ ] Complete the tax-free purchase registration at checkout; an ordinary receipt alone is not the full procedure.
- [ ] Keep receipts and the purchased goods available for departure customs checks. Check the airport process before handing over checked baggage containing the goods.
- [ ] Record the full amount paid in the budget; record a refund only when received.

Restaurant meals, café drinks consumed during the trip, and hotel stays are not exported shopping goods. Do not budget them as tax-free purchases. [JNTO tax-free shopping overview](https://www.japan.travel/en/plan/japans-tax-exemption/)

### 💰 Shopping allowance

Keep the current **US$300 shopping and miscellaneous allocation** initially:

| Purpose | Suggested cap USD |
| --- | ---: |
| Clothes | 150 |
| Traditional souvenirs | 80 |
| Stationery and small gifts | 40 |
| Laundry, wrapping, and other small extras | 30 |
| **Total** | **300** |

If clothing becomes a bigger priority, a **$500 allowance** would raise planned spending from **$3,850 to $4,050** and leave **$950 of the $5,000 available**. This is an optional scenario, not a change to the accepted working allocation. Premium textiles, handmade ceramics, or several garments may need a larger dedicated allowance.

Try clothes before purchasing, keep receipts and care labels, and leave room in the suitcase. Ask about packing for fragile items and distinguish mass-produced souvenirs from craft pieces by checking the maker and materials. Decide on three priority keepsakes before shopping; this helps keep the purchases meaningful and the budget controlled.

## 🍵 Buying good-quality matcha

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

**Recommendation: buy your main matcha souvenir in Kyoto; use Tokyo as an equally practical backup.** Kyoto offers a rewarding specialist-shop outing, with Uji as an optional tea-focused excursion. Buying in Kyoto does not automatically make the powder better: compare the producer, product, intended preparation, packaging and freshness. Ippodo has shops in both cities, so Tokyo can supply tea from the same company. [Official Ippodo locations](https://www.ippodo-tea.co.jp/pages/store-locator)

### 📍 Matcha shopping stops

#### Ippodo Kyoto main store — first choice

**Location and how to get there:** 52 Tokiwagi-cho, Nakagyo-ku. Kyoto Shiyakusho-mae Station, exit 11, approximately 5 minutes on foot; combine with a central Kyoto outing. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Ippodo+Tea+Kyoto+Main+Store)

**Current visiting details:** Store 10:00–17:00; normally closed the second Wednesday of the month. Ask staff to recommend a small pack for drinking plain or for lattes. [Official store](https://global.ippodo-tea.co.jp/pages/store-kyoto?view=store-detail)

#### Marukyu Koyamaen Nishinotoin / Motoan — another Kyoto specialist

**Location and how to get there:** Nishinotoin-dori, just south of Oike, Nakagyo-ku. Start from Karasuma Oike Station and follow the map west toward Nishinotoin; allow roughly 10 minutes as a planning estimate. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Marukyu+Koyamaen+Motoan+Kyoto)

**Current visiting details:** Shop 09:30–18:00; café last order 17:00, normally closed Wednesdays, with holiday exceptions. Product availability and prices must be checked in store. The official site currently says tea classes are suspended. [Official shop](https://www.marukyu-koyamaen.co.jp/motoan-shop/about/)

#### Ippodo Shin-Marunouchi — Tokyo backup

**Location and how to get there:** Shin-Marunouchi Building B1, 1-5-1 Marunouchi, Chiyoda-ku. Tokyo Station Marunouchi north exit, about 2 minutes on foot, with underground access. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Ippodo+Shin+Marunouchi)

**Current visiting details:** Shop 11:00–21:00, Sundays/holidays until 20:00; takeout finishes earlier. Use the current Shin-Marunouchi branch: older guides may list the former Marunouchi shop. [Official branch](https://www.ippodo-tea.co.jp/pages/store-shin-marunouchi)


### 💰 What to buy and what to budget

- **Verified price example:** Ippodo's Japanese site currently displays **Ikuyo-no-mukashi, 20g, ¥1,512**. This is a current October 2026 reference, not a guaranteed 2027 price. [Japanese shop](https://www.ippodo-tea.co.jp/)
- **Suggested allowance:** set aside **¥2,000–5,000 for one small pack**, depending on the tea staff recommends. This is a spending target rather than a quoted price for every brand. Start with one or two small sealed packs instead of a large souvenir stash.
- Tell staff whether you want **plain whisked matcha (usucha)** or **matcha for milk drinks**. Ask for a recommendation within your price limit; paying the most does not guarantee your preferred taste.
- Choose pure matcha when you want tea to whisk at home; check ingredients to avoid accidentally buying a sweetened drink mix. Ippodo's **Uji-Shimizu** is a different sweetened product.
- Check the best-before date and follow the maker's storage instructions. Keep the pack sealed during the trip and avoid heat and moisture. Buy from the maker or an established retailer, and check purchase limits and stock before making a special journey.

Prices are changing: Ippodo announced product and pricing revisions for October/November 2026. Recheck the Japanese domestic shop and branch before departure rather than relying on an old screenshot or an overseas shipping price. [Official revision notice](https://global.ippodo-tea.co.jp/blogs/news/20260901)

**Fit into the budget:** pay for matcha from the existing traditional-souvenir allowance, replacing another souvenir if needed. Keep café drinks under the food/café allowance and powder under shopping, so the same purchase is not counted twice. An Uji excursion is optional and should replace another outing rather than crowd the blossom itinerary.

## 👖 Thrift and vintage shopping in Tokyo and Kyoto

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

These are secondhand fashion shops; designer vintage can cost more than new basics. Stock, sizes and prices vary, so the amounts below are personal spending limits rather than advertised shop prices.

| Stop | Address and access | Why visit / current hours |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **2nd STREET Shimokitazawa, Tokyo** | Fine Court Shimokitazawa 2F, 2-30-13 Kitazawa, Setagaya-ku. Take the Odakyu line from Shinjuku or Keio Inokashira line from Shibuya to Shimokitazawa, then walk using the map. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=2nd+Street+2-30-13+Kitazawa) | A starting point for used clothing and a walk around Shimokitazawa's vintage shops. Current hours 11:00–21:00. Several branches exist; this address identifies the intended one. [Official branch](https://www.2ndstreet.jp/shop/details?shopsId=31139) |
| **2nd STREET Shijo Kawaramachi, Kyoto** | Kyogoku Toho Building 2F, 538-1 Nakanocho, Nakagyo-ku, on Shin-Kyogoku just north of Shijo. Arrive at Hankyu Kyoto-Kawaramachi Station and walk into the shopping streets. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=2nd+Street+Shijo+Kawaramachi+Kyoto) | Convenient during a downtown Kyoto shopping afternoon; compare everyday pieces and branded items. Current hours 11:00–21:00. [Official branch](https://www.2ndstreet.jp/shop/details?shopsId=30860) |
| **RAGTAG Kyoto** | 379-2 Naramonocho, Shimogyo-ku, Shijo-dori west of Teramachi. Official access: 2-minute walk from Kyoto-Kawaramachi Station or 7 minutes from Gion-Shijo. [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=RAGTAG+Kyoto+379-2) | Best for browsing Japanese designer labels and a special garment; allow for higher prices. Men's and women's departments. Current hours 11:00–20:00, year-end/New Year closures. [Official store](https://www.ragtag.jp/real-store/0000000030) |

### 🧭 Practical shopping plan

Use a flexible Tokyo afternoon for Shimokitazawa, and combine the two Kyoto stores with the existing Kawaramachi/Teramachi shopping stop. Keep shopping flexible if blossoms or light are particularly good that day.

Set an initial **¥2,000–5,000 ceiling for a shirt** and **¥5,000–12,000 for a jacket**; these are search targets, and many designer pieces exceed them. Try clothes on where permitted, inspect seams, stains and zippers, and ask about returns before paying. Budget within the existing **US$150 clothing allocation**; choose one memorable garment rather than adding purchases to the reserve. Recheck branch hours shortly before the 2027 trip.

## 🎥 Cameras, content creation and livestreaming

[[#🧭 Contents|⬆️ Back to contents]]

**Your goal:** high-quality travel videos for Instagram, TikTok, Facebook and YouTube, plus livestreams on those platforms. You plan to buy an **iPhone 18 Pro Max** as your main creation device; the separate equipment budget is still to be confirmed. These are buying options, not a purchase decision. Equipment is separate from the current US$5,000 Japan budget unless you explicitly decide otherwise.

**Updated recommendation:** start with your planned **iPhone 18 Pro Max, a compatible wireless microphone, grip and power bank**. Test this kit before buying an additional camera; the Pocket and mirrorless options below remain optional alternatives. Use the phone for mobile livestreams. Choose a mirrorless system if creative lens control, photography and carefully produced videos justify the extra weight and editing. A higher-priced camera does not automatically improve a platform-compressed live feed.

### 🎒 Light versus heavier setups

All complete-kit weights and US-dollar budget ranges below are planning estimates, including a few accessories but excluding a computer. Actual configurations, local Chilean prices, tax and stock need checking before purchase. Manufacturer camera weights are identified separately.

| Setup | Carrying weight and equipment allowance | Best use / tradeoff |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **📱 Existing phone + wireless mic + grip** | Roughly 0.5–0.9 kg with phone, power bank and accessories; **US$150–400 added equipment**, excluding a new phone. | Simplest starting kit, fast editing and native-platform mobile lives. Quality depends on the phone, light, lens and audio; use the rear main camera when practical. |
| **🪶 DJI Osmo Pocket 4 + mic + phone** | Camera **190.5 g**; roughly 0.7–1.2 kg for camera, phone, mic, grip, power bank and cables. Allow **US$700–1,000** for the camera kit and accessories, excluding phone. | My travel-vlog pick: mechanically stabilized walking shots with little setup. Fixed-lens limitations and no waterproofing; phone remains the default live device. |
| **⚖️ Sony ZV-E10 II + one compact wide zoom** | Camera **377 g including battery/card**, before lens. Roughly 1.0–1.8 kg for a compact kit; allow **US$1,500–2,300**. | More lens flexibility, strong recorded-video potential and USB streaming to a computer. Start with a compact wide lens or kit zoom; compare selfie framing after stabilization crop. Walking footage needs more technique/support than a pocket gimbal. |
| **🎬 Sony α7 V + 24–70mm f/2.8 zoom** | Camera **695 g including battery/card**, before lens. Roughly 2–3.5 kg with lens, batteries, mic and bag; allow **US$4,500–6,500**. | A premium hybrid option for deliberate video and blossom photography. Much more expensive and tiring on long days; a big zoom may be too tight for arm-length selfies. A tripod or gimbal adds further weight. |

**Verified reference prices, checked October 2026:** a DJI European store displayed Pocket 4 Standard Combo at **€449**; Sony US displayed α7 V body only at **US$2,899.99**. These are regional references, not Chile/Japan quotes or guaranteed 2027 prices. Pocket 4 offers a 1-inch sensor, three-axis stabilization and 10-bit D-Log; its advertised 4K/240fps is a **slow-motion mode**, not a livestream specification. [DJI current store](https://store.dji.com/ch/product/osmo-pocket-4?from=events-seasonal-specials-2026) · [Pocket 4 weight/specification reference](https://www.dji.com/au/support/product/osmo-pocket-4) · [Sony α7 V store](https://electronics.sony.com/imaging/interchangeable-lens-cameras/all-interchangeable-lens-cameras/p/ilce7m5-b)

[ZV-E10 II official specifications](https://helpguide.sony.net/ilc/2430/v1/en/contents/232h_specifications_ilc2430.html) · [α7 V official specifications](https://www.sony.co.uk/electronics/support/e-mount-body-ilce-7-series/ilce-7m5/specifications)

### 🎙️ Accessories worth buying first

- **Audio:** a compatible wireless clip-on mic with wind protection, such as a DJI Mic-series kit. Get the receiver/phone adapter needed for your exact phone and camera; a transmitter-only camera bundle may not include it. Do not assume Bluetooth audio works in every live app. Check DJI's phone and app compatibility lists, then test each app. [Mic compatibility and downloads](https://www.dji.com/mic-mini/downloads) · [Connection guidance](https://www.dji.com/mic-mini/faq)
- **Support:** a small grip/mini tripod first; add a full tripod for controlled shots or a gimbal only after testing whether you need it. Mounting hardware must fit the chosen camera.
- **Power:** a suitable USB-C power bank, cables and spare removable camera batteries where applicable. Verify charging while recording and microphone connection at the same time.
- **Storage:** manufacturer-recommended cards, a card reader and a backup drive. For long trips, copy footage to two separate locations before deleting cards; built-in storage alone is not a backup.
- **Light:** a small LED for seated hotel narration; daylight and positioning usually help more outdoors. An ND filter is optional for controlling shutter speed in bright daylight.

### 📐 High-quality recorded videos for all four platforms

Record a clean **4K master** where the camera supports it, usually 25/30fps for ordinary travel scenes and 50/60fps when motion or intended slow-down justifies it. These are creative starting settings, not platform mandates. Test indoor lighting for flicker and adjust exposure/shutter locally.

Capture **horizontal 16:9 for long YouTube videos**, and dedicated **vertical 9:16 takes for TikTok, Instagram/Facebook Reels and YouTube Shorts**. Horizontal footage cropped vertically loses much of the scene; take both versions of important moments and leave room for captions. Use clean lenses, stable shots, clear voice audio and controlled highlights. Ten-bit/log can help grading when supported, but requires a suitable editing workflow; use a normal color profile until comfortable with grading. Keep exports free of another platform's watermark and upload the original export separately.

For YouTube's SDR 4K uploads, the official recommended bitrate is **35–45 Mbps at standard frame rates** or **53–68 Mbps at high frame rates**. These are recorded upload recommendations, not mobile livestream targets. [YouTube export settings](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171?hl=en)

### 📡 Livestreaming: mobile walks versus controlled broadcasts

**Walking in Japan:** phone → native YouTube/TikTok/Instagram/Facebook app → cellular connection. Add the tested mic, grip and power bank. Start at 720p/30 or 1080p/30 where available, and choose the setting your connection sustains. A stable stream with clear audio is preferable to repeated interruptions at a higher resolution. Mobile plans must support tethering if other equipment needs it; check data allowances and throttling.

**Seated high-quality broadcasts:** mirrorless camera → USB webcam mode or compatible clean-HDMI capture device → laptop/streaming software → platform. Use a tripod, light, tested audio path, power and reliable upload. Sony specifically documents ZV-E10 II USB livestreaming; HD/4K modes need the specified USB connection and compatible computer. [Sony USB streaming guide](https://www.sony.com/electronics/support/e-mount-body-zv-e-series/zv-e10m2/articles/00345861)

**Camera-to-phone wireless streaming:** support is model-specific. DJI confirms Pocket 3 RTMP streaming, but do not assume Pocket 4 has an identical workflow or that either feeds every native social app. Confirm the chosen model's current manual and test the exact destination before buying it for live use. [Pocket 3 RTMP support](https://www.dji.com/support/product/osmo-pocket-3)

**All platforms at once:** this requires additional software/service routing, platform access and often separate vertical/horizontal layouts. A single camera purchase does not provide universal simultaneous broadcasting. Start with one live destination and publish edited videos across the others; if simultaneous lives become essential, validate every account's encoder/stream-key access before selecting the service.

### 🔓 Platform access to check before departure

| Platform | What to verify on your own account |
| --- | --- |
| **YouTube** | Native mobile live requires at least **50 subscribers**, channel verification and other eligibility conditions. First activation can take up to 24 hours; mobile audience limits can apply below 1,000 subscribers. Desktop/encoder eligibility follows a different route. [Official mobile requirements](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/9228390?hl=en) |
| **TikTok** | Check current age, regional follower eligibility and access to LIVE. Desktop LIVE Studio access is a separate check; do not assume a stream key is available just because native mobile LIVE works. Some official help pages were inaccessible during this audit. [Official LIVE help](https://support.tiktok.com/en/live-gifts-wallet/tiktok-live/what-is-tiktok-live) · [LIVE Studio help](https://www.tiktok.com/live/studio/help) |
| **Instagram** | Check whether your account can start native Live and whether Live Producer is available for external software. Official pages were inaccessible, so exact current thresholds were not independently verified here. [Official Live Producer guide](https://about.instagram.com/blog/tips-and-tricks/instagram-live-producer) |
| **Facebook** | Test native Live or Live Producer on the intended Page/profile. Its official requirement page redirected to login, so check eligibility directly in your account before relying on this workflow. [Official Facebook Live help](https://www.facebook.com/help/1636872026560015) |

**Connectivity planning:** at 6 Mbps video bitrate, an hour uses approximately **2.7 GB for video alone**, plus audio and overhead. Test sustained upload at the location, leave headroom and keep a local recording when supported. [YouTube live encoder recommendations](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/2853702?hl=en)

### 🤝 Best software for iPhone plus Pocket 4

**My pick for editing footage from both cameras is LumaFusion**, especially for a finished YouTube travel film with deliberate audio and color work. **CapCut is my pick for fast vertical social posts.** These are workflow recommendations; compatibility with every recording mode on your exact phone must be tested.

| Task | iPhone 18 Pro Max | DJI Osmo Pocket 4 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Record | Apple Camera for quick automatic shots; Blackmagic Camera for manual control. | Record on the camera; use **DJI Mimo** for the supported device connection and workflow. Blackmagic Camera does not replace the Pocket's recording interface. |
| Edit a finished vlog | **LumaFusion** on iPhone, with the original phone clips. | Transfer the original clips to the iPhone, then import into the same LumaFusion project. |
| Make quick social clips | **CapCut** on iPhone. | Transfer camera clips, then edit in CapCut; Mimo is another option for a quick DJI workflow. |
| Go live while walking | Native platform app first; PRISM/Moblin when account access and setup permit. | Continue using the phone as the default live camera. Do not assume Pocket 4 can feed those apps merely because recorded files can be edited there. |

**Transfer:** use the supported Mimo workflow or the appropriate USB/card-reader route to import originals, rather than repeatedly exporting and recompressing finished clips. Pocket 4 specifications list MP4/HEVC footage; test an original clip in your chosen editor, particularly 10-bit/log or high-frame-rate modes. [DJI Mimo](https://www.dji.com/mimo) · [Official footage export guide](https://repair.dji.com/help/content?customId=01700006849&lang=en&paperDocType=ARTICLE&re=US&spaceId=17) · [Pocket 4 specifications](https://www.dji.com/au/support/product/osmo-pocket-4)

**Match the look:** for the easiest combined edit, start with normal SDR color and the same frame rate on both cameras. For log footage, apply the correct conversion separately to each camera before matching color; do not apply a DJI D-Log conversion to Apple Log. LumaFusion offers color controls and LUT import, but you should test your exact files before travel. [LumaFusion listing](https://apps.apple.com/cl/app/lumafusion/id1062022008)

### 🔄 A repeatable iPhone creation workflow

1. **Record:** start with 4K/30fps for ordinary travel clips, where available. Capture separate vertical and horizontal takes of key scenes; use a tested wireless mic for talking shots.
2. **Color:** begin with a normal SDR workflow for straightforward sharing. Use Apple Log/other supported log modes only when you have tested the color conversion and editing pipeline; unconverted log will look flat. HDR also needs a consistent export/upload workflow. Do not assume the same options are available in every app.
3. **Edit:** make one clean vertical master for social platforms, then upload separately to each. Build the longer horizontal vlog in LumaFusion. Keep an export without platform-specific music or watermarks; music availability/licensing differs by destination.
4. **Go live:** start at 720p/30 or 1080p/30 where supported, then adjust to sustained upload performance. A local 4K recording and a live feed are different outputs. Check native live or encoder access on each account before a simultaneous broadcast.
5. **Back up:** copy original footage daily to a second location. Log/ProRes, where supported, can consume much more storage than ordinary compressed video; test a day's recording volume before choosing capacity and external storage.

**Before the trip:** record, edit, export and upload one horizontal and one vertical test video. Then run a realistic live session on each destination, checking audio input, chat, orientation, heat, battery, data usage and reconnect behavior. Confirm Chilean App Store availability, current subscriptions, and app support after iOS updates. Keep software subscriptions and phone/accessory costs separate from the Japan spending plan.

### ✅ Buying decision and pre-trip test

Your planned phone is **iPhone 18 Pro Max**. Confirm your preferred editing device and **separate accessory/software ceiling** before adding equipment. Buy early enough to practice. Run a realistic outdoor session and a longer seated stream on each intended platform: check microphone selection, wind noise, focus, framing, upload stability, heat, battery consumption and backup recording. Keep camera/software/streaming subscriptions and extra data outside the existing trip estimate until deliberately budgeted.
